Blank when the attribution read failed.; **FLAGS** — 'reverted <date> → <value>' / 'partly reverted …' (v4.600: the booked change was undone and the saving left the run-rate; listed again under Reverted savings; 'partly reverted <date> (co-attributed <SETTING> → <value>)' when a saving-direction change the same scan saw with it was undone, or 'reverted <date> via co-attributed <SETTING> → <value>; <own SETTING> since fully undone' once its own setting was fully undone later; an auto-suspend of NULL or ≤0 reads 'never'), 'volume-confounded', 'cheaper but slower' (REGRESSED yet saved), 'performance regressed', 'performance unjudged' (NO_BASELINE / INSUFFICIENT_AFTER), 'co-attributed $0' (LBA-1), joined with ' · '.; **VERIFIED_AT** — Verification time, account-local.

### Pipeline
What is ahead: the addressable opportunity plus queued work, projected with measured haircuts (the old Scenarios tab, rebuilt). Addressable $ scopes to **Company and Window** (divided by the window's real day span); queued work is every open Action Center item for the Company, items filed under ALL included (not windowed); the measured slider defaults are account-wide. Verified savings never enter the projection; they live on Proof ▸ Proof.

| Metric | Means | Formula | Unit | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Addressable $/mo** | The de-duplicated idle-timer opportunity, the same basis as the Cost ▸ Optimization & Savings default total. Right-sizing is added only when the "Include right-sizing (mart profile)" toggle is on; unread maintenance (v4.604, #35) only when Cost ▸ Optimization & Savings ▸ Storage & waste confirmed it against access history this session, for this Company with the Database filter clear, with that panel last shown under 1h ago (real time), no Refresh and no credit-rate change since (the last 30 complete days of maintenance, less objects already booked as of the scan's ledger read, which is cached for up to 5m; one booked in another session keeps counting until the scan is re-run at least 5m after that booking, at most 1h 5m); and storage waste (v4.605) only when that section's storage-waste scan ran this session for this Company with read evidence, under the same 1h / Refresh rules and no storage-rate change since (tables nobody read in 90 days: a stale table's active bytes, or the Time Travel a 1-day retention would release on a table still written, a monthly run-rate only while it keeps being written, less tables already booked); a table counted by both counts once (the larger wins). The delta reads 'N opportunities · idle-timer only', or the counted levers joined with ' + ' (e.g. 'idle timer + right-sizing + unread maintenance + storage waste'). The headline follows the levers counted (Cost ▸ Optimization & Savings' rule): when the efficiency mart cannot be read or has no metering in the window it still shows the other levers' total (e.g. confirmed unread maintenance), with the delta adding 'idle timer not counted (efficiency mart unavailable)' or '(no warehouse metering in this window)', and it is '—' only when no counted lever has an item; a failed read's unavailable state says idle-timer savings are not sized. A 'Levers counted / Not counted' caption under the KPIs names each lever and why one is missing; when unread maintenance is not counted, a 'Check unread maintenance → Cost ▸ Optimization & Savings ▸ Storage & waste' doorway (can_open-gated) seeds that pill; when it is counted but storage waste is not, a 'Check storage waste → Cost ▸ Optimization & Savings ▸ Storage & waste' doorway seeds the same pill. | rollup_savings(idle_opportunities(idle_advisor(with_auto_suspend_settings(eff_idle)))) [+ resize_opportunities(size_recommendations(with_warehouse_settings(eff_sizing)))] [+ savings_rollup.unread_lever(session handoff).opportunities] [+ savings_rollup.storage_lever(session handoff, rate = STORAGE_USD_PER_TB_MONTH from the settings the page shell already loaded).opportunities]; each opportunity is confidence-weighted and the same warehouse, or the same table, is counted once (the larger wins); zero reads for the unread and storage legs | USD per month | mart27_sql.eff_idle_analysis -> MART_WAREHOUSE_EFFICIENCY_DAILY (mart-only, hourly tier, shared with Cost ▸ Optimization & Savings; no live fallback) + SHOW WAREHOUSES (shared metadata read); optional mart27_sql.eff_sizing_profile |
| **Queued work $/mo** | Every open Action Center item for the Company (an item filed under ALL counts in every Company), normalized to a monthly run-rate. Queued estimates are summed as authored: all-users AI budget items tracked from different Company views overlap each other (see Cost ▸ Chargeback & AI ▸ Exceptions). The delta reads 'N open · M unpriced · K one-time' (+ '· N no period', and '· top N projected' when the queue is longer than the 500 rows the projection uses). | Uncapped SQL window totals over the open queue: QUEUED_MONTHLY_TOTAL (MONTHLY x1, ANNUAL ÷12; ONE_TIME and a NULL / '' PERIOD are kept OUT of the run-rate but counted), OPEN_TOTAL, UNPRICED_TOTAL (NULL or ≤ 0 estimate), ONE_TIME_TOTAL, NO_PERIOD_TOTAL; decision.monthly_equivalent shapes only the projected rows | USD per month | workbench_sql.action_center(company, False, 500, with_totals=True) -> ACTION_QUEUE (recent tier; the "queue" cache salt refreshes it after a write) |
| **Settling** | Change-scan ledger rows still inside their 14-day measured window (the same figure as on Proof ▸ Proof). | auto_settle_pending_count | count | Reused from the memoized proof reads (zero extra reads) |
| **Expected capture $/mo** | What the addressable plus queued pipeline is expected to yield after the adoption and realization haircuts, with a low–high band. It reads 'No evidence' when nothing clears the confidence floor, 'Below floor' (v4.606) when priced open items exist but none clears it, and 'Unpriced' when candidates exist but carry no dollars. A caption counts the priced open items the floor leaves out and their $/mo: those with no authored confidence (e.g. an AI exception, whose estimate is projected spend, not a saving) apart from those below the floor (decision.floor_exclusions, de-duplicated by entity like the projection). | scenario_projection(pipeline_frame(rollup.items, queued)): entities de-duplicated (largest estimate wins, so a queued action on the same warehouse as an addressable opportunity counts once); gross = Σ per-entity estimate; expected = gross x adoption x realization; low = gross x adoption x max(realization − 0.2, 0); high = min(gross, gross x adoption x min(realization + 0.2, 1)) | USD per month | logic.decision.pipeline_frame + scenario_projection |
| **Adoption % / Realization % / Confidence floor (sliders, measured defaults)** | Haircuts on the projection. Each slider's help names its source: 'measured: …', or 'assumed — nothing decided yet'. "Reset to measured" restores the defaults. | Defaults: adoption = the Acted-on rate (ACCEPTANCE_PCT), else 60 (also 60 when the acceptance read failed, labelled as an assumed placeholder, v4.606); realization = the Realization rate, else the carried realization, else 70; confidence floor = 0.6 (policy). Each key is seeded into session state only when absent. The projection runs in a fragment and never re-reads the proof signals, so a slider move costs 0 reads. | percent / ratio 0-1 | st.slider inside an @st.fragment; inputs to scenario_projection |
| **(caption) 500-action cap** | The projection covers the top 500 open actions; the cap is disclosed when it binds. | len(queue) vs the LIMIT 500 | count | workbench_sql.action_center(..., 500) |
| **(caption) N open actions not touched in 30+ days** | Stale open actions whose estimate is decaying. | SUM(STALE); STALE = UPDATED_AT < account_now − 30d | count | logic.workbench.stale_planning |
| **(caption) N actions on watched entities** | Open actions whose source entity is on the viewer's watchlist, pinned within their severity band. | SUM(WATCHED) via mark_watched_pairs(SOURCE_ENTITY_TYPE, SOURCE_ENTITY_KEY) | count | USER_WATCHLIST (recent tier) |

*Columns (pipeline table):* **WATCHED** — ★ when the source entity is on the viewer's watchlist.; **KIND** — 'Addressable' (a synthetic row from the Cost ▸ Optimization & Savings rollup: entity `WAREHOUSE:<target>`, title `Tighten auto-suspend on <wh>` or `Right-size <wh>`; an unread-maintenance row is entity `OBJECT:<fqn>`, title `Stop maintenance on unread <fqn>`, so it de-duplicates against a queued OBJECT action on the same FQN and never against a warehouse; a storage-waste row is also `OBJECT:<fqn>`, titled `Archive or drop unread <fqn>` or `Cut Time Travel retention on unread <fqn>`) or 'Queued' (an ACTION_QUEUE row).; **SEVERITY / TITLE** — The queued action's severity and title (blank severity on addressable rows).; **SOURCE_ENTITY_TYPE / SOURCE_ENTITY_KEY** — The entity the row targets, which is the de-duplication key.; **CONFIDENCE** — One 0-1 bar: the opportunity's confidence weight on addressable rows, the authored confidence on queued rows (the help names both).; **MONTHLY_USD** — $/mo run-rate: the opportunity's $/mo, or the queued estimate normalized by PERIOD ('—' for one-time, unspecified or unpriced); the authored figure is kept as AUTHORED_USD.; **PERIOD** — MONTHLY / ANNUAL / ONE_TIME / unspecified.; **OWNER** — The accountable owner of a queued action.; **STALE (flag)** — An open action untouched for 30+ days (stale_planning). A row click opens the row's source entity in Control Room ▸ Entity 360 when it is an Entity 360 type (an AI_BUDGET scope item does not open), only for profiles that can open Control Room. "Track more query work → Operations ▸ Optimize" sits below the table, gated on Operations access.

## Decision Studio (renamed Proof in v4.597): where each tab went
Decision Studio is now **Proof**. Old saved default views, `?page=decision-studio[&section=…]` deep links and in-app navigation requests all remap through `navigate.LEGACY_TARGETS` (a deep link whose target page is off the viewer's profile falls back to Proof). APP_USAGE and APP_QUERY_TELEMETRY record the page as "Decision Studio" before the release and "Proof" after, so Admin usage panels show both rows through the transition.

| Old tab | Now | What changed |
|---|---|---|
| **Scorecard** | Proof ▸ Proof | Merged with ROI (Pays for itself, Acted on, Alert precision, On solid evidence). Every headline total now comes from SQL (the ROI tile used to sum a capped frame). |
| **ROI** | Proof ▸ Proof | Merged with Scorecard (Verified savings run-rate, Added this quarter, Realization rate, the charts). "Open pipeline" is replaced by Settling; the priced pipeline is Proof ▸ Pipeline. Per-item evidence, attribution classes and carried realization are new. |
| **Portfolio** | Operations ▸ Optimize | The fix queue: every family gets a specific diagnosis and first fix, and one-click Track / Track all ACT NOW into Action Center (see Operations ▸ Optimize). |
| **SLOs** | Operations ▸ Pipeline SLA ▸ Tonight (built-in objectives) | **Retired:** the SLO cockpit and the create-objective editor are gone. SLO_OBJECTIVES and SP_SLO_BREACH_SCAN are untouched, so any ACTIVE row still raises PERF_SLO_BREACH and badges the Entity 360 watchlist (older event text still says "Decision Studio → SLOs"). Change or retire a row in Snowsight (see the PERF_SLO_BREACH playbook). The two built-in objectives need no setup. |
| **Products** | Hidden | **Hidden:** the data-product economics body is kept in `app/ui/decision_studio.py` but is not reachable from the UI, so its live ACCESS_HISTORY read is gone from the app. It can be revived later as derived products. |
| **Cost Truth** | Cost ▸ Spend & Attribution (grain coverage caption) | The ratio line moved (inside "Load company attribution"). The four-lens inventory table is no longer rendered: the billed lens is the Spend credit tiles; metered, measured and allocated are defined in the grain-coverage row. |
| **Scenarios** | Proof ▸ Pipeline | Adds the addressable $ (idle timer; right-sizing, confirmed unread maintenance and unread-table storage waste when counted), normalizes queued estimates to monthly, defaults the sliders to measured values, and runs the projection in a fragment (a slider move is 0 reads). |
| **Experiments** | Proof ▸ Proof (evidence rows) | **Retired:** the Experiments board and the Action Center "Start optimization experiment" expander are gone. OPTIMIZATION_EXPERIMENTS and SP_VERIFY_EXPERIMENT stay; an EXPERIMENT ledger row still appears on Proof as 'Experiment (verified by hand)'. |
| **Control Room ▸ Decision Studio (older link)** | Operations ▸ Optimize | Remapped. |

## Alerts
*7 sections · 31 metrics.*

### Page header + Open-events KPI tiles
Top-of-page triage headline: how many alert events are currently open, split by severity. Company-scoped to the current filter (that company's rows PLUS account-level COMPANY='ALL' rows). These tiles are the uncapped source of truth; the feed table below is capped at 500.

| Metric | Means | Formula | Unit | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Open critical** | Count of currently OPEN or ACK events whose severity is CRITICAL, for the selected company (+ account-level). | COUNT_IF(UPPER(SEVERITY)='CRITICAL') over STATUS IN ('OPEN','ACK'); tile shows int(CRIT). severity 'bad' if >0 else 'ok', delta_color inverse if >0. | count | mart_sql.open_alert_severity_counts(company) -> ALERT_EVENTS.SEVERITY/STATUS/COMPANY (live, uncapped aggregate). Fallback: derived from the 500-row open_alert_events feed if the aggregate fails (labeled: a caption says the tiles were counted from the capped feed). v4.606: once that feed is full the counts are floors — Open total reads '500+', and Open critical / Open high carry '+' unless the feed also holds a less severe row (it is severity-first, so then the count is exact). |
| **Open high** | Count of OPEN/ACK events with severity HIGH, same company scope. | COUNT_IF(UPPER(SEVERITY)='HIGH'); severity 'warn' if >0 else 'ok'. | count | open_alert_severity_counts -> ALERT_EVENTS (live). |
| **Open total** | All OPEN+ACK events across every severity (incl. MEDIUM/LOW), same company scope. The feed below only shows the 500 most-severe/newest, so this can exceed the visible rows. | COUNT(*) over STATUS IN ('OPEN','ACK') + company predicate. | count | open_alert_severity_counts -> ALERT_EVENTS (live). |

### Delivery status banner (_delivery_status)
Answers 'who gets paged at 2am?' in a single green/red banner. Verifies that at least one ENABLED route names a notification integration that actually exists AND that TASK_ALERT_NOTIFY is started. Rendered on BOTH the Open events section and the Native delivery section.

| Metric | Means | Formula | Unit | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Delivery LIVE / suspended / missing-integration (banner)** | LIVE when an enabled route's named integration exists and TASK_ALERT_NOTIFY state='started'. Warns if integration exists but the task is suspended. Errors if enabled routes point at a non-existent integration. Refuses to claim 'missing' if the metadata probes themselves failed (#32). v4.606: 'suspended' only when SHOW TASKS reads that state; a failed SHOW TASKS read says delivery is unable to verify (UNKNOWN, not proof of a suspended task), and an ok read with no visible row or state says the task's state is not visible to the app's role; the task read refreshes every 5 minutes (recent tier, was the 4-hour metadata tier). | want = DISTINCT UPPER(INTEGRATION_NAME) from ENABLED ALERT_ROUTES; have = names from SHOW NOTIFICATION INTEGRATIONS; has_integ = bool(want & have); task_state from SHOW TASKS LIKE 'TASK_ALERT_NOTIFY'. | status | run('SHOW NOTIFICATION INTEGRATIONS'), run('SHOW TASKS LIKE TASK_ALERT_NOTIFY'), run(ALERT_ROUTES WHERE ENABLED) — all metadata/recent tier. |
| **last send (banner suffix)** | Timestamp of the most recent notified event, appended to the LIVE banner. | MAX(NOTIFIED_AT), truncated to first 16 chars (YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM). | timestamp | run(SELECT MAX(NOTIFIED_AT) AS LAST_SEND FROM ALERT_EVENTS) — live. NOTE: this is ALERT_EVENTS.NOTIFIED_AT, a DIFFERENT source than the 'Last alert delivery' card below (ALERT_DELIVERIES.SENT_AT). |

### Last alert delivery card (_last_delivery_card)
Separates 'quiet' from 'broken': one KPI row + verdict line built PER ENABLED ROUTE, aggregating the worst route state. Rendered on BOTH Open events and Native delivery sections.

| Metric | Means | Formula | Unit | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Last alert delivery** | How long ago the newest confirmed send went out across ANY route ('never' if no route has ever sent). 'ok' if <48h, 'warn' if older, 'bad' if never. | mins = MIN over routes of MINUTES_SINCE = DATEDIFF('minute', LAST_SENT_AT, now); value = humanize_duration(mins,'min')+' ago'. sev = ok if mins<2880(48h) else warn; 'bad'/'never' if no LAST_SENT_AT. delta = newest LAST_SENT_AT string[:16]. | duration (min -> humanized) / timestamp delta | mart_sql.last_delivery_health() -> ALERT_DELIVERIES.SENT_AT (per ROUTE_ID) joined with ALERT_ROUTES/ALERT_EVENTS/ALERT_CONFIG/APP_ERROR_LOG (live). |
| **Eligible to send now** | Open events inside the sender's 24h window matching an enabled route and not yet in that route's delivery ledger — summed ACROSS routes (an event matching 2 routes counts twice, mirroring the per-route sender). | waiting = int(sum(ELIGIBLE_NOW)) across route rows. ELIGIBLE_NOW = COUNT(DISTINCT event) OPEN, RAISED_AT>=now-24h, family/company/severity match, NOT EXISTS delivery for this route. severity 'warn' if any STUCK route. | count | last_delivery_health() eligible CTE -> ALERT_EVENTS x ALERT_ROUTES x ALERT_CONFIG minus ALERT_DELIVERIES (live). |
| **Send failures (24h)** | Count of route_send_failed log rows in the last 24h summed across enabled routes. Red only when a route's LATEST failure is newer than its latest success (recovery-aware). | fails = int(sum(ROUTE_FAILS_24H)); ROUTE_FAILS_24H = COUNT_IF(LOGGED_AT>=now-24h) of APP_ERROR_LOG rows PAGE='NotifyWebhook', ERROR_TYPE='route_send_failed', ROUTE_ID = SPLIT_PART(CONTEXT,' ',2). severity 'bad' if any FAILING_NOW else 'ok'. | count | last_delivery_health() fails CTE -> APP_ERROR_LOG.CONTEXT/ERROR_TYPE (live). |
| **Verdict line (caption)** | Plain-English worst-route diagnosis: no-route / FAILING_NOW (endpoint refusing, CONSEC_FAILS since last success) / STUCK (undelivered backlog older than 90m) / queued / genuinely quiet. | Priority: routes_n==0 -> 'no enabled route'; else worst of FAILING_NOW (rank by CONSEC_FAILS), STUCK (rank by ELIGIBLE_NOW+EXPIRED_UNDELIVERED backlog, oldest past 24h window), waiting>0 -> 'N queued', else 'quiet'. | text | last_delivery_health() FAILING_NOW/STUCK/CONSEC_FAILS/EXPIRED_UNDELIVERED columns (live). |
| **Last send failure (expander)** | Most-recent failure error message for the worst failing route, truncated to 400 chars. | LAST_FAILURE = MAX_BY(LEFT(ERROR_MESSAGE,200), LOGGED_AT) of the failing route. | text | last_delivery_health() fails CTE -> APP_ERROR_LOG.ERROR_MESSAGE. |

### Open events feed + drawer + bulk + snoozed
The triage feed: severity-then-newest sorted open/ack events, with a per-event drawer (detail, rule, delivery, playbook, re-check, history, prior resolutions, AI evidence, investigate/fix nav, ack/resolve/snooze with audit), an optional storm rollup, an operator bulk lifecycle tool, and a Snoozed events drawer. Feed capped at 500 rows.

| Metric | Means | Formula | Unit | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Group by rule (storm view) toggle** | Collapses many events of one rule into a single row (e.g. 5 warehouses over budget = 1 row). Display-only rollup; dedupe semantics untouched. | pandas groupby RULE_ID -> EVENTS=count, WORST=max severity rank, NEWEST=max RAISED_AT, SAMPLE=first TITLE. | count/derived | derived in alerts.py from open_alert_events feed (no separate query). |
| **Re-check condition now (result)** | Re-runs THIS rule's condition on the same basis the alert uses, for the guessed warehouse/account, and compares to the rule threshold: 'clear' if current<threshold, 'still over' otherwise. Credits and the cloud-services ratio read today (since account midnight); query fail %, and since v4.606 queued time and remote spill, read the trailing 24h (queued/spill: per warehouse from FACT_QUERY_HOURLY, the basis SP_ALERT_SCAN arms [04]/[05] and their auto-clear use — the old since-midnight QUERY_HISTORY read could say 'Condition clear' in the morning while the scan still saw the event over). v4.606: a daily-credit alert raised for a closed day (an earlier date in its title) reads 'Today so far … a partial day cannot clear a full-day total' — never 'Condition clear' and no ACTIONED prefill; only 'still over' is evidence. | current_v = CURRENT_VALUE from recheck_sql.recheck_sql(rule_id, warehouse, company); thr = rule THRESHOLD_NUM from alert_rules; compares current_v<thr. | metric-dependent (varies by rule; shown as X,.2f, no unit), except PERF_QUEUED_MINUTES ('queued time (24h)' since v4.606, was 'queued time today'), whose value and threshold are minutes and read Hr/Min/Sec (v4.605: 'queued time (24h) = 2h 25m vs threshold 30m'); when rounding makes both sides read the same, the sentence names the gap ('1h 30m vs threshold 1h 30m, 54s over' / ', 300ms under'; a gap of 59.5s or more between two texts that read the same minute is capped at '59s', never '1m') or says the value is at the threshold ('1h 30m, at the threshold'); the prefilled resolve note carries the same text | recheck_sql.recheck_sql -> live re-check (today, or the trailing 24h for PERF_QUERY_FAIL_PCT / PERF_QUEUED_MINUTES / PERF_SPILL_GB — FACT_QUERY_HOURLY); threshold from mart_sql.alert_rules() -> ALERT_CONFIG. |
| **Respond — closed loop: Tighten auto-suspend to 60s (drawer, operators)** | The drawer's auto-suspend fix for the warehouse an alert names. Tighten-only: it never raises a timer already at 1-60s (an info note, no SQL). v4.606: the guard reads ONE warehouse's SHOW WAREHOUSES row on the 30-second live tier, never the shared 4 h 'jump_wh' cache, so a timer a DBA tightened in a worksheet since is not loosened; a failed read, an unsafe name or no exact-name row leaves the setting unknown — a warning and no ALTER. A NULL auto_suspend on the listed row is the KNOWN never-suspend setting (0), the same as on Cost Intelligence ▸ Optimization & Savings (Remediation & ledger, Idle & sizing), so it gets the enable-a-timer ALTER. | remediation.tighten_suspend_plan(current, known, target=60): unknown -> no SQL; 0 < current <= 60 -> no SQL; otherwise ALTER WAREHOUSE <wh> SET AUTO_SUSPEND = 60 (a timer above 60s, or a never-suspend 0). Execute = typed warehouse name, a REMEDIATION_LOG row (FINDING_TYPE ALERT_CLOSED_LOOP) and a $0 ESTIMATED SAVINGS_LEDGER row (FINDING_TYPE AUTO_SUSPEND) whose NOTES say the daily change scan adopts it only for a decrease from a positive timer; an enabled never-suspend timer is not auto-booked (verify it on the Savings ledger). | seconds | recheck_sql.warehouse_settings_sql (SHOW WAREHOUSES LIKE, live tier, probe) -> insights.auto_suspend_in_force / show_warehouse_settings / show_auto_suspend; remediation.tighten_suspend_plan + closed_loop_note_suffix; execute_statement + REMEDIATION_LOG + SAVINGS_LEDGER |
| **Respond — closed loop: Statement timeout 1h (drawer, operators)** | The drawer's one-click 1h cap for the warehouse an alert names. Tighten-only (v4.601, F1): the warehouse's value in force is read live first and a cap that would loosen or keep it is a note, not SQL. Impact-weighed (v4.603, #33 D1): when the SET would tighten, ONE warehouse's last 30 days of completed statements are read and a cap that would have cancelled any of them (or an impact that could not be read) is a warning; the ALTER is withheld until the operator ticks the explicit override, then the typed-name confirm executes it. A verified zero is an info note and the ALTER shows directly. | OVER_TARGET_RUNS = COUNT_IF(SUCCESS AND TOTAL_ELAPSED_TIME > 3600000) (includes queue and compile time: an upper bound); EXEC_OVER_TARGET_RUNS = the same on EXECUTION_TIME alone; MAX_ELAPSED_SEC = longest completed statement; 30 trailing days, UPPER(WAREHOUSE_NAME) = the warehouse. A failed read renders 'unavailable' with its error and never reads as none. | count / seconds | ops_sql.warehouse_stmt_timeout_sql (live tier, probe); ops_sql.warehouse_timeout_impact -> QUERY_HISTORY (historical tier, probe, only when the SET would tighten); logic.stmt_timeout.tighten_timeout_plan; execute_statement + REMEDIATION_LOG + $0 ESTIMATED SAVINGS_LEDGER row |
| **Snoozed (N) drawer** | Currently-snoozed events hidden from the triage feed until their wake time, with operator early-un-snooze. | rows where STATUS='SNOOZED', ORDER BY SNOOZED_UNTIL, same company predicate as feed. v4.606: the read is LIMIT 100, so a full tray reads '100+' with a caption ('Showing the 100 soonest to wake'), never the snoozed total. | count | mart_sql.snoozed_alert_events(100, company) -> ALERT_EVENTS (V086 SNOOZE_* cols, probe-gated, live). |

*Columns:* **RAISED_AT** — When the scan raised the event (account-time, tz-converted; the real sortable column). ALERT_EVENTS.RAISED_AT.; **AGE** — Display-only 'how long ago' companion to RAISED_AT ('3h ago'), computed in-app via humanize_age(RAISED_AT, account_now()).; **SEVERITY** — CRITICAL/HIGH/MEDIUM/LOW; drives the severity-first sort order. ALERT_EVENTS.SEVERITY.; **COMPANY** — Owning company or ALL (account-level). ALERT_EVENTS.COMPANY.; **TITLE** — Human alert title from the scan. ALERT_EVENTS.TITLE.; **STATUS** — OPEN or ACK (feed filters to these two). ALERT_EVENTS.STATUS.; **Ack by** — Display name of the acker, resolved from ACK_BY via with_user_names(). Empty if not acked.; **ACK_BY** — Raw acker identity (role/user) kept alongside the resolved name. ALERT_EVENTS.ACK_BY.; **DETAIL (drawer)** — Full alert detail text, rendered as plain text (not markdown) to prevent injection. ALERT_EVENTS.DETAIL.; **METRIC_VALUE** — The numeric value the alert fired on (fetched in the feed payload; feeds the recheck/precision comparison). ALERT_EVENTS.METRIC_VALUE.; **Rollup: EVENTS** — Number of open events collapsed under that RULE_ID in storm view.; **Rollup: NEWEST** — Most recent RAISED_AT within the rolled-up rule group.; **Rollup: SAMPLE** — First event TITLE as a representative sample for the group.; **Drawer history (events_for_rule): RAISED_AT/SEVERITY/COMPANY/TITLE/STATUS** — Last 20 events (<=180d) for this rule so the operator sees how often it fires; v4.606: at the 20-row cap the expander reads 'latest 20 events', not a 90-day total. mart_sql.events_for_rule(rule_id,90) -> ALERT_EVENTS.; **Prior resolutions: RESOLVED_AT** — When this rule was previously closed. ALERT_EVENTS.RESOLVED_AT (STATUS='RESOLVED', excl machine closes SUPERSEDED/AUTO_CLEARED/SNOOZE_SUPPRESSED/CONDITION_ENDED).; **Prior resolutions: RESOLUTION_KIND** — ACTIONED/NOISE/EXPECTED tag from the prior close. ALERT_EVENTS.RESOLUTION_KIND.; **Prior resolutions: RESOLUTION_NOTE** — The prior resolve/remediate note. Sourced from ALERT_AUDIT.NOTE (MAX_BY over ACTED_AT, ACTION IN RESOLVE/REMEDIATE/NOTE), NOT ALERT_EVENTS — the earlier ALERT_EVENTS.RESOLUTION_NOTE read raised 000904 and was fixed 2026-08-17.; **Snoozed: SEVERITY/TITLE/SNOOZED_UNTIL/SNOOZE_BY/SNOOZE_REASON** — Snoozed event, its server-computed wake time, who snoozed it and why. ALERT_EVENTS SNOOZE_* columns.

### Rules — config table + precision + suggested thresholds + generator
Account-wide alert rule configuration (thresholds are data in ALERT_CONFIG, not code) plus a fixed 90-day per-rule precision scorecard, data-driven threshold suggestions, and a generate-only UPDATE builder. v4.606: the builder seeds New threshold and Enabled from the picked rule's current ALERT_CONFIG values and writes only the columns that change (toggling Enabled no longer writes THRESHOLD_NUM = 0.0; an unchanged pick generates nothing and says so), and warns when a threshold of 0 would make the rule fire on every row. A failed precision / suggestion read renders unavailable with the error (needs-setup only when ALERT_EVENTS is truly absent or unreadable). Company filter does NOT apply here.

| Metric | Means | Formula | Unit | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Precision % (per rule)** | How often a rule's fires were real: share of ACTIONED among ACTIONED+NOISE closes over 90d. EXPECTED (maintenance/known) excluded from the denominator; high UNTAGGED means the score isn't trustworthy yet. | ROUND(100*ACTIONED/NULLIF(ACTIONED+NOISE,0),1); ACTIONED=COUNT_IF(RESOLUTION_KIND='ACTIONED'), NOISE=COUNT_IF(...='NOISE'), over STATUS='RESOLVED', RAISED_AT>=now-90d. | percent (%.1f%%) | mart_sql.rule_precision(90) -> ALERT_EVENTS.RESOLUTION_KIND (recent tier). |
| **Suggested threshold (per rule)** | A data-driven threshold that keeps >=90% of ACTIONED alerts while cutting NOISE, direction-aware per rule (inverse-metric rules tuned downward). Advice only. | tuning.suggestions_by_rule(rule_metric_kinds df, current thresholds): per-rule suggest_threshold over that rule's ACTIONED/NOISE METRIC_VALUE distribution; current threshold from ALERT_CONFIG.THRESHOLD_NUM. v4.606: the BASIS counts 'tagged resolutions with a metric value' (untagged, EXPECTED and NULL-metric closes are not in it — it used to say 'All N resolved events'); UNTAGGED_N rides through per rule, and when a rule's untagged closes outnumber its tagged ones the BASIS adds 'Caveat: N more closed untagged; tag them before trusting this.' | metric-dependent (rule's own metric) | mart_sql.rule_metric_kinds(90) -> ALERT_EVENTS.METRIC_VALUE/RESOLUTION_KIND; app-side math in app/logic/tuning.py. |

*Columns:* **RULE_ID** — Rule identifier. ALERT_CONFIG.RULE_ID.; **FAMILY** — Rule family (COST/SECURITY/PIPELINE/...), used for routing. ALERT_CONFIG.FAMILY.; **NAME** — Human rule name. ALERT_CONFIG.NAME.; **ENABLED** — Whether the scan evaluates this rule. ALERT_CONFIG.ENABLED.; **SEVERITY** — Severity the rule raises at. ALERT_CONFIG.SEVERITY.; **THRESHOLD_NUM** — Numeric firing threshold (raw, rule-specific unit). ALERT_CONFIG.THRESHOLD_NUM.; **WINDOW_HOURS** — Informational only — the caption warns the actual scan window is fixed in SP_ALERT_SCAN and editing this column does not change the scan. ALERT_CONFIG.WINDOW_HOURS.; **OWNER** — Rule owner. ALERT_CONFIG.OWNER.; **CHANNEL** — Legacy per-rule channel hint. ALERT_CONFIG.CHANNEL.; **UPDATED_AT** — Last edit timestamp. ALERT_CONFIG.UPDATED_AT.; **Precision: RESOLVED_EVENTS** — Resolved events for the rule in 90d excluding machine closes. COUNT_IF(RESOLUTION_KIND NOT IN (SUPERSEDED/AUTO_CLEARED/SNOOZE_SUPPRESSED/CONDITION_ENDED)).; **Precision: ACTIONED** — Resolved as a real fix. COUNT_IF(RESOLUTION_KIND='ACTIONED').; **Precision: NOISE** — Threshold cried wolf. COUNT_IF(RESOLUTION_KIND='NOISE').; **Precision: EXPECTED** — Known/maintenance; excluded from precision denominator. COUNT_IF(RESOLUTION_KIND='EXPECTED').; **Precision: UNTAGGED** — Resolved with no kind (pre-V021 or closed untagged); high value = score not trustworthy. COUNT_IF(RESOLUTION_KIND IS NULL).; **Suggested: CURRENT_THRESHOLD** — The rule's current THRESHOLD_NUM. From ALERT_CONFIG via the thresholds dict.; **Suggested: SUGGESTED_THRESHOLD** — Recommended threshold from the rule's own ACTIONED/NOISE metric distribution. tuning.suggest_threshold.; **Suggested: NOISE_N / ACTIONED_N** — Counts of NOISE / ACTIONED resolved events the suggestion is based on.; **Suggested: BASIS** — Plain-English basis for the suggestion (which percentile/separation it used).

### History — events-by-day chart, alert MTTA/MTTR, incident lifecycle, delivery SLO, route table, route backlog, alert fatigue
Retrospective process health at fixed 30/90-day horizons. Company filter applies ONLY to incident lifecycle metrics (all other panels are account-wide). The incident lifecycle row shows MTTA, Time to mitigate, MTTR and Alerts / incident only: Reopen rate and Change-correlated were removed (round-4 hunt ALC-1; v4.351) because no writer persists INCIDENTS.REOPENED_FROM or a WH_CHANGE / DEPLOY incident member, so each read a permanent 0%; change correlation lives in the Control Room RCA. v4.606: a failed read on any panel renders unavailable with the error (needs-setup only for a true absence), never its 'appears once …' wording; the route backlog's 'No route has an undelivered backlog' all-clear needs a successful read with every enabled route at 0 (an ok read with no rows means no enabled route).

| Metric | Means | Formula | Unit | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **events-by-day (stacked bar)** | Daily count of raised alert events over 30d, stacked by severity. | COUNT(*) GROUP BY DATE(RAISED_AT), SEVERITY over RAISED_AT>=CURRENT_DATE-30. y-axis 'Alert events' = sum(Events); color=Severity (shared palette). | count/day | mart_sql.alert_event_history(30) -> ALERT_EVENTS; chart charts.events_by_day. |
| **MTTA (last 4 active weeks)** | Mean time from raise to acknowledge, averaged over the last 4 weeks that had any ack. Alert grain (per event). | Weekly MTTA_MIN = ROUND(AVG(DATEDIFF('minute', RAISED_AT, ACK_AT)),1); app takes mean of the last 4 non-null weekly values then humanize_duration(...,'min'). Mean-of-weekly-means (unweighted). | minutes -> humanized (h/m) | mart_sql.alert_mttr(90) -> ALERT_EVENTS.RAISED_AT/ACK_AT (recent tier). |
| **MTTR (last 4 active weeks)** | Mean time from raise to resolve, last 4 weeks with resolves, alert grain. Excludes machine closes (SUPERSEDED/AUTO_CLEARED/SNOOZE_SUPPRESSED/CONDITION_ENDED). | Weekly MTTR_MIN = ROUND(AVG(IFF(RESOLUTION_KIND NOT IN (SUPERSEDED/AUTO_CLEARED/SNOOZE_SUPPRESSED/CONDITION_ENDED), DATEDIFF('minute',RAISED_AT,RESOLVED_AT), NULL)),1); app means last 4 non-null weeks, humanized. | minutes -> humanized | mart_sql.alert_mttr(90) -> ALERT_EVENTS lifecycle timestamps. |
| **Events (90d)** | Total alert events raised in the last 90 days. | int(sum(weekly EVENTS)); weekly EVENTS=COUNT(*). Comma-grouped. | count | mart_sql.alert_mttr(90) -> ALERT_EVENTS. |
| **MTTA (90d) [incident grain]** | Median detected -> FIRST human response on AUTO-declared incidents only (Next-Fifty #12a, owner decision O-6: a manual declare is itself the response). The response is the Control Room Acknowledge, Mark mitigated, or a single-incident Close, each of which back-fills INCIDENTS.ACK_AT only when empty (the bulk reset does not). Help text shows the sample size (ACKED_N). Company-scoped (that company + ALL). | MEDIAN(DATEDIFF('minute',DETECTED_AT,ACK_AT)) over the 90d window WHERE ACK_AT IS NOT NULL AND ACK_AT >= DETECTED_AT AND DECLARED_BY = 'SP_INCIDENT_AUTODECLARE'; humanize_duration; NULL -> '—'. | minutes -> humanized | mart_sql.incident_metrics(90, company) MTTA_MIN / ACKED_N -> INCIDENTS (recent). |
| **Time to mitigate (90d) [incident grain]** | Median detected -> MITIGATED: an operator's Mark mitigated, or (V154) the auto-mitigate sweep in SP_INCIDENT_AUTODECLARE, which stamps MITIGATED_AT = the last member alert's resolve time (not its own run time). Company-scoped. | MEDIAN(DATEDIFF('minute',DETECTED_AT,MITIGATED_AT)) WHERE MITIGATED_AT IS NOT NULL AND MITIGATED_AT >= DETECTED_AT; humanize_duration; NULL -> '—'. | minutes -> humanized | mart_sql.incident_metrics(90, company) MTTM_MIN -> INCIDENTS (recent). |
| **MTTR (90d) [incident grain]** | Median detected -> resolved at INCIDENT grain (not per event). Company-scoped (that company + ALL). | MEDIAN(DATEDIFF('minute',DETECTED_AT,RESOLVED_AT)); humanize_duration. | minutes -> humanized | mart_sql.incident_metrics(90, company) MTTR_MIN -> INCIDENTS (recent). |
| **Alerts / incident** | Storm compression: how many alert members each incident absorbs. Higher = storms collapse into one object. | ROUND(ALERT_MEMBERS/NULLIF(N_incidents,0),1); ALERT_MEMBERS=COUNT of INCIDENT_MEMBERS MEMBER_KIND='ALERT' joined to windowed incidents. | ratio (1 decimal) | mart_sql.incident_metrics -> INCIDENTS x INCIDENT_MEMBERS. |
| **Events delivered (30d)** | Raised events that got at least one delivery row, over raised events (30d). Not expected to be 100% since routes filter by severity. | EVENTS_DELIVERED = COUNT(events raised in 30d that JOIN a delivery row with SENT_AT in 30d) / EVENTS_RAISED = COUNT(events raised 30d). Shown 'X / Y' at %.0f. | count / count | mart_sql.delivery_slo_summary(30) -> ALERT_DELIVERIES + ALERT_EVENTS (recent). |
| **Latency p50 / p95** | Median and 95th-pct minutes from raise to first send, for delivered events (30d). | MEDIAN / APPROX_PERCENTILE(...,0.95) of DATEDIFF('second',RAISED_AT,FIRST_SENT)/60.0; humanize_duration(...,'min'). FIRST_SENT=MIN(SENT_AT) per event. | minutes -> humanized | mart_sql.delivery_slo_summary(30) -> ALERT_DELIVERIES.SENT_AT, ALERT_EVENTS.RAISED_AT. |
| **Undelivered criticals (30m+)** | OPEN CRITICAL events raised >=30 min ago with zero delivery rows — criticals that never left the building. | COUNT(e LEFT JOIN d) WHERE SEVERITY='CRITICAL' AND STATUS='OPEN' AND d.EVENT_ID IS NULL AND RAISED_AT<=now-30m. severity 'bad' if >0. | count | delivery_slo_summary -> ALERT_EVENTS/ALERT_DELIVERIES. |
| **Route failures (30d)** | Integration-level send failures in 30d, counted as route-days: a route that keeps failing counts once per day, not once per run. | COUNT(DISTINCT SPLIT_PART(CONTEXT,' ',2) \|\| day) FROM APP_ERROR_LOG WHERE PAGE='NotifyWebhook' AND ERROR_TYPE='route_send_failed' AND LOGGED_AT>=now-30d — v4.606: keyed on the route id (CONTEXT token 2), not the whole CONTEXT text, so one route writing two message suffixes (the drain's retry note and V164's escalation re-post) or renamed mid-day still counts once that day. %.0f. | count | delivery_slo_summary -> APP_ERROR_LOG. |
| **Expired-undelivered (30d)** | undelivered_expired rows SP_NOTIFY_WEBHOOK raises when an eligible OPEN event crosses 24h unsent — a persistent outage, not lag. | COUNT(*) FROM APP_ERROR_LOG WHERE ERROR_TYPE='undelivered_expired' AND LOGGED_AT>=now-30d. severity 'bad' if >0. | count | delivery_slo_summary -> APP_ERROR_LOG. |
| **Alert fatigue (per rule)** | Attention-cost sheet: which rules fire a lot without earning it. | per RULE_ID over 30d: EVENTS=COUNT(*), PER_WEEK=COUNT(*)/(days/7), ACTIONED/NOISE/EXPECTED via COUNT_IF, UNTAGGED=resolved with empty kind, REPEAT_EVENTS=COUNT(*)-COUNT(DISTINCT COALESCE(DEDUPE_KEY,EVENT_ID)). | counts + events/week | mart_sql.alert_fatigue(30) -> ALERT_EVENTS (recent). |

*Columns:* **MTTA/MTTR table: WEEK** — Week bucket (DATE_TRUNC('week',RAISED_AT)). ALERT_EVENTS.; **MTTA/MTTR table: EVENTS** — Events raised that week. COUNT(*).; **MTTA/MTTR table: ACKED** — Events acked that week. SUM(IFF(ACK_AT IS NOT NULL,1,0)).; **MTTA/MTTR table: RESOLVED** — Human resolves that week, excluding machine closes. SUM(IFF(RESOLVED_AT IS NOT NULL AND KIND NOT IN (SUPERSEDED/AUTO_CLEARED/SNOOZE_SUPPRESSED/CONDITION_ENDED),1,0)).; **MTTA/MTTR table: MTTA_MIN / MTTR_MIN** — Weekly average minutes raise->ack / raise->resolve; humanized in the table via styled_table duration auto-format.; **delivery_by_route: ROUTE_ID** — Route identifier. ALERT_DELIVERIES.ROUTE_ID.; **delivery_by_route: SENDS** — Total send rows for the route in 30d. COUNT(*).; **delivery_by_route: EVENTS** — Distinct events the route delivered in 30d. COUNT(DISTINCT EVENT_ID).; **delivery_by_route: LAST_SENT** — Most recent SENT_AT for the route. MAX(SENT_AT).; **route_backlog: ROUTE_ID / INTEGRATION_NAME** — Enabled route and its integration name. ALERT_ROUTES.; **route_backlog: BACKLOG** — OPEN events (<=24h) matching the route's family/company/severity, not yet delivered to it. COUNT(ev.EVENT_ID) with NOT EXISTS delivery.; **route_backlog: OLDEST_MIN (shown 'Oldest')** — Age of the oldest pending event for the route, minutes (humanized). NULL if empty backlog. ROUND(DATEDIFF('second',MIN(RAISED_AT),now)/60,1).; **alert_fatigue: RULE_ID** — Rule identifier. ALERT_EVENTS.RULE_ID.; **alert_fatigue: EVENTS** — Events in 30d. COUNT(*).; **alert_fatigue: Events/week (PER_WEEK)** — Weekly firing rate. COUNT(*)/(days/7), 1 decimal.; **alert_fatigue: ACTIONED / NOISE / EXPECTED** — Resolution-kind mix (case-insensitive COUNT_IF).; **alert_fatigue: UNTAGGED** — Resolved with no kind. COUNT_IF(STATUS='RESOLVED' AND kind='').; **alert_fatigue: REPEAT_EVENTS** — Duplicate fires collapsed by dedupe key. COUNT(*)-COUNT(DISTINCT COALESCE(DEDUPE_KEY,EVENT_ID)).; **alert_fatigue: LAST_RAISED** — Most recent raise for the rule. MAX(RAISED_AT).

### Native delivery — status, last-delivery card, routing table, templates
Account-wide delivery configuration: repeats the delivery-status banner and last-delivery card, shows the family->channel routing table, and offers the native email + webhook SQL templates for download.

| Metric | Means | Formula | Unit | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Delivery status banner** | Same LIVE/suspended/missing banner as on Open events (see that section). | see _delivery_status. | status | SHOW NOTIFICATION INTEGRATIONS / SHOW TASKS / ALERT_ROUTES / ALERT_EVENTS.NOTIFIED_AT. |
| **Last alert delivery card** | Same per-route quiet-vs-broken KPI row as on Open events (see that section). | see _last_delivery_card. | duration/count | mart_sql.last_delivery_health(). |

*Columns:* **routes: ROUTE_ID** — Route identifier. ALERT_ROUTES.ROUTE_ID.; **routes: FAMILY** — Family the route matches ('ALL' matches everything). ALERT_ROUTES.FAMILY.; **routes: MIN_SEVERITY** — Minimum severity the route forwards. ALERT_ROUTES.MIN_SEVERITY.; **routes: INTEGRATION_NAME** — Snowflake NOTIFICATION INTEGRATION the route targets. ALERT_ROUTES.INTEGRATION_NAME.; **routes: ENABLED** — Whether the route is active. ALERT_ROUTES.ENABLED.; **routes: Created by** — Display name resolved from CREATED_BY via with_user_names(). ALERT_ROUTES.CREATED_BY.; **routes: CREATED_AT** — Route creation timestamp. ALERT_ROUTES.CREATED_AT.

## Security
*9 sections · 45 metrics.*

### Decision queue (Security decision queue + Operational governance)
Exceptions-first landing view: an evidence-qualified per-domain posture score row, the materialized security exception queue with Action Center / Entity 360 drills, a change-risk noise diagnostic, and (below) the governance-drift score + posture trend. This is where an operator triages 'what needs a decision today.'

| Metric | Means | Formula | Unit | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **<Domain> KPI tile (Identity / Privilege / Change risk / Data movement / Trust center)** | Per-domain posture score 0-100 (higher=better), shown only when that domain's evidence source reports COMPLETE coverage; otherwise shows '--' with a state word (On demand / Not configured / Unknown). Delta shows '<state> \| <N> open' exceptions. | logic/security.domain_posture: score = max(0,100 - min(100, penalty)); penalty = sum over exceptions of _SEVERITY_PENALTY[severity]{CRITICAL25/HIGH12/MEDIUM5/LOW2/INFO0} * min(3,max(1,IMPACT_COUNT)). findings = sum of IMPACT_COUNT (clip>=1) — v4.606: the shown 'N open' (and the page verdict's 'N open finding(s)' and the 'Decision queue (n)' badge, TOTAL_ROWS) read the uncapped per-domain totals DOMAIN_FINDINGS / DOMAIN_ROWS computed before the per-domain 100-row cap, so ~4,000 queued CHANGE RISK rows no longer read '100 open'; the penalty still scores the capped rows (it saturates), and a caption says 'Showing N of M queued exceptions' per capped domain. state Healthy>=90/Watch>=70/Act else. Coverage from security_domain_coverage contract. | score 0-100 (+ open-exception count) | security_sql.security_exception_queue -> V_SECURITY_EXCEPTION_QUEUE (core); security_sql.security_domain_coverage -> MART_SECURITY_POSTURE_DAILY + SOURCE_FRESHNESS_STATE. Domain scoring in logic/security.py. Probe-gated (V075): the 'available after V075' setup state shows only when BOTH reads are absent; a failed exception-queue read (a timeout, a missing column, or an absent queue beside a readable coverage contract) renders unavailable with the error and no domain posture (v4.605). A readable queue beside a coverage-contract read that failed other than by absence renders every tile '--' plus an unavailable line with the error; an empty queue then says the coverage is unknown (never 'not complete'), and the page verdict adds 'domain coverage could not be read, so no domain is scored'. |
| **Destructive events (7d) / By TF_* roles / No role attributed / On app's own DB (change-risk diagnostic expander)** | Diagnostic breakdown of the DESTRUCTIVE change-risk flood: total DROP/TRUNCATE events at RISK_SCORE>=70 in 7d, and the % share driven by Terraform service roles (TF_*), by unattributed drops, and by events on DBA_MAINT_DB. | security_sql.change_risk_destructive_breakdown(7): COUNT(*) where CHANGE_KIND='DESTRUCTIVE' AND RISK_SCORE>=70 AND EVENT_TS>=now-7d, grouped by role/db/schema/QUERY_TYPE/DROP_CLASS; UI computes pct = 100*events_subset/total. ROLE_CLASS via LIKE 'TF~_%' ESCAPE '~'. DROP_CLASS = 'identity / policy' when QUERY_TYPE ILIKE ANY('%USER%','%ROLE%','%POLICY%') or the whitespace-normalized preview opens with one of the 17 V151 keep openers (DROP USER / ROLE / DATABASE ROLE / APPLICATION ROLE / (kind) POLICY), else 'other object'; a caption reports TF_IDENTITY_POLICY_EVENTS (pre-LIMIT window total of TF_* identity / policy drops the queue keeps once V151 is applied). | count and % of total | security_sql.change_risk_destructive_breakdown -> FACT_SECURITY_CHANGE (core, hourly). security_center._render_change_risk_diagnostic. |
| **Governance drift score (/100) + Deductions (Operational governance panel)** | Countable hygiene debt as a single 0-100 number with named deductions. Deductions = number of penalty drivers that fired. State Healthy/Watch/Act. If some inputs did not resolve, the score is labeled a CEILING ('incomplete'). | logic/governance.governance_drift: score = round(max(0,100 - sum(capped penalties))). Per-driver penalty = count*weight, capped: MFA gaps 5pt/user cap25; Expired cred 8pt cap24; Expiring cred 2pt cap10; Break-glass grants 6pt cap18; No auto-suspend 3pt cap12. Weights SETTINGS-overridable (resolve_gov_weights). Missing input contributes 0 (surfaced as 'incomplete'). | score 0-100 | Inputs preferred from mart27_sql.security_posture(90) -> MART_SECURITY_POSTURE_DAILY latest day (METRIC MFA_GAP_USERS/EXPIRED_CRED/EXPIRING_CRED_10D/BREAKGLASS_GRANTS_30D/WH_NO_AUTOSUSPEND); fallback security_sql.governance_counts (live USERS+CREDENTIALS+GRANTS_TO_USERS) and SHOW WAREHOUSES for autosuspend. |
| **Posture trend line (90d) — selectable METRIC** | Direction of a chosen posture metric over 90 days (e.g. EXPIRING_CRED_10D), with a first->latest delta caption. Renders only after loader has 2+ days. | charts.daily_metric_line over MART_SECURITY_POSTURE_DAILY VALUE by DAY for the selected METRIC; caption arrow = up/down/flat of (latest-first). | metric count over time | Shares the single mart27_sql.security_posture(90) read used by the governance score. _posture_trend_panel. |

*Columns:* **SEVERITY** — Exception severity (CRITICAL/HIGH/MEDIUM/LOW) from V_SECURITY_EXCEPTION_QUEUE; drives sort and the domain penalty weight.; **DOMAIN** — Which security domain the exception belongs to (IDENTITY/PRIVILEGE/CHANGE RISK/DATA MOVEMENT/TRUST CENTER).; **TITLE / DETAIL** — Human summary and evidence detail of the exception.; **IMPACT_COUNT** — Affected-entity count; multiplies the domain penalty up to 3x and is summed into 'open findings'.; **STATUS / OWNER** — Workflow status and assigned owner of the exception.; **SCOPE** — Derived in UI (_scope_label): 'ACTOR to OBJECT' when actor/object companies differ, else object or actor company, else COMPANY or 'ALL'.; **DETECTED_AT** — Timestamp the exception was materialized/detected.; **ENTITY_TYPE / ENTITY_KEY / ACTION_ID** — Drill keys: map to Entity 360 (entity_type normalized via _action_entity_type) and Action Center tracked action.; **ROLE_NAME / DATABASE_NAME / SCHEMA_NAME / QUERY_TYPE / DROP_CLASS / EVENTS / USERS / LAST_SEEN / ROLE_CLASS (diagnostic table)** — Change-risk breakdown grain: actor role, session db/schema, Snowflake query type, identity / policy vs other object (the V151 keep list), destructive-event count, distinct users, most-recent event, and TF_* vs other classification.

### Access
Identity and role hygiene evidence: MFA gaps with real password-login proof, failed logins and their reasons, privileged/break-glass holders, new networks for admins, expiring credentials, dormant + reawakened accounts, unused roles, window grants, plus effective-access graph and admin-grant timing. Includes the Auditor export pack.

| Metric | Means | Formula | Unit | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Users needing MFA now** | Active users who password-logged-in within the last 30 days but have no native MFA. SSO/key-pair-only users excluded. | TOTAL_USERS_WIN = COUNT(*) OVER () before the LIMIT 200 (v4.606: the badge counted the 200 rows; a caption now says 'Showing the N users with the most password logins of M'). SQL users_without_mfa: USERS (DELETED_ON NULL, not DISABLED, HAS_PASSWORD, NOT HAS_MFA) JOIN FACT_LOGIN_DAILY where SUM(PASSWORD_LOGINS) over last 30d >0. Live fallback users_without_mfa_live counts LOGIN_HISTORY rows FIRST_AUTHENTICATION_FACTOR='PASSWORD' & IS_SUCCESS. When neither path proved posture the section stripe is neutral, not green (v4.606). | count of users | security_sql.users_without_mfa -> ACCOUNT_USAGE.USERS + DBA_MAINT_DB.OVERWATCH.FACT_LOGIN_DAILY (mart-first); fallback users_without_mfa_live -> USERS + LOGIN_HISTORY. |
| **Failed logins table (no KPI)** | Per-user failed login attempts in the window (reader capped at 30 days; v4.606: under calendar presets too — logic.security.capped_window keeps the last 31 days of the range, so a whole calendar month fits and 'Current year' is no longer a ~273-day scan under a 30d label; the clean state names the window served). | security_sql.failed_logins: COUNT(*) FAILED_ATTEMPTS, COUNT(DISTINCT CLIENT_IP), MAX(EVENT_TIMESTAMP), MAX_BY(ERROR_MESSAGE) where IS_SUCCESS='NO'. Fact path failed_logins_fact: SUM(FAILURES), MAX_BY(ERROR_CATEGORY), served only when FACT_SECURITY_LOGIN_DAILY is dense over exactly the window served (security_login_fact_coverage(days, bounds); v4.606 — one 90-day density read used to gate a 7- or 30-day window). | counts | security_sql.failed_logins -> ACCOUNT_USAGE.LOGIN_HISTORY; fact failed_logins_fact -> FACT_SECURITY_LOGIN_DAILY. |
| **Privileged role holders table (no KPI)** | Current holders of SNOW_ACCOUNTADMINS / SNOW_SYSADMINS. | admin_role_holders: GRANTS_TO_USERS where ROLE IN (SNOW_ACCOUNTADMINS,SNOW_SYSADMINS) AND DELETED_ON NULL. | rows (holders) | security_sql.admin_role_holders -> ACCOUNT_USAGE.GRANTS_TO_USERS. |
| **Failed-login reasons (network policy vs credentials)** | Failed logins grouped by reason, split into NETWORK POLICY vs CREDENTIAL/OTHER. | failed_login_reasons: COUNT(*), COUNT(DISTINCT USER_NAME), COUNT(DISTINCT CLIENT_IP), category = IFF(ERROR_MESSAGE ILIKE '%network%','NETWORK POLICY','CREDENTIAL / OTHER'). 30d cap to match failed_logins (held under calendar presets too, v4.606). | counts | security_sql.failed_login_reasons -> LOGIN_HISTORY; fact failed_login_reasons_fact -> FACT_SECURITY_LOGIN_DAILY (ERROR_CATEGORY). |
| **New networks** | Count of admin-role (user,IP) pairs first seen inside the window and not seen in the 90 days before it (90-day baseline). A pair quiet 90+ days re-flags by design. v4.606: the baseline is the 90 days BEFORE the window starts (it was a fixed last 90 days, so a 90-day or wider window had no baseline and listed every routine admin IP as new); the window is capped at the last 90 days (calendar presets too) so a full baseline precedes it, and the caption says so when it cuts. | TOTAL_PAIRS_WIN = COUNT(*) OVER () before the LIMIT 200 (v4.606; was len(nn.df)). new_network_logins: admins=GRANTS_TO_USERS(SNOW_ACCOUNTADMINS/SNOW_SYSADMINS); hist=LOGIN_HISTORY for those users from window start − 90 days (at most 180 days back); first_seen=MIN(EVENT_TIMESTAMP) per (user,ip); row surfaces where FIRST_SEEN is inside the window; LOGINS / SUCCESSES count the pair's logins inside the window. The fact twin serves only when FACT_SECURITY_LOGIN_DAILY is dense over window + 90 days (180 at a 90-day window); backfill_365.sql loads those 180 days, where it loaded 90 before v4.606. | count of (user,IP) pairs | security_sql.new_network_logins -> GRANTS_TO_USERS + LOGIN_HISTORY; fact new_network_logins_fact -> FACT_SECURITY_LOGIN_DAILY. |
| **Expiring <=10d / Already expired** | Credentials expiring within 10 days (still active) and credentials already past expiry but still active. | cdf=expiring_credentials(10). expired=count(STATUS=='EXPIRED'); 'Expiring <=10d'=len(cdf)-expired. SQL: EXPIRATION_DATE<=now+10d, STATUS=IFF(EXPIRATION_DATE<now,'EXPIRED','EXPIRING'), DAYS_TO_EXPIRY=DATEDIFF(day,now,EXPIRATION_DATE). | count of credentials | security_sql.expiring_credentials -> ACCOUNT_USAGE.CREDENTIALS. |
| **Dormant users / High severity** | Enabled users dormant 90+ days still holding roles; High = 180+ days dormant OR 5+ roles. | dormant_users(90): USERS not deleted/disabled, LAST_SUCCESS_LOGIN NULL or <now-90d, CREATED_ON<now-90d; DAYS_DORMANT=DATEDIFF(day, COALESCE(LAST_SUCCESS_LOGIN, CREATED_ON), now) (v4.606: days since creation for a user who never logged in, no longer 9999), NEVER_LOGGED_IN flags those and they lead the order (NEVER_LOGGED_IN, DAYS_DORMANT, ROLE_COUNT desc; LIMIT 300); role rollup via GRANTS_TO_USERS. Severity in logic/insights.dormant_severity (High if never logged in, DAYS_DORMANT>=180 or ROLE_COUNT>=5, Medium>=90, else Low). | count of users | insights_sql.dormant_users -> ACCOUNT_USAGE.USERS + GRANTS_TO_USERS. Toggle-gated (heaviest scan). |
| **Reawakened accounts / High severity** | Long-dormant accounts that just logged in (signal LAST_SUCCESS_LOGIN can't show). High = 180+ day silence OR 5+ roles. | dormant_reawakening(): 365d LOGIN_HISTORY successes, LAG->PREV_LOGIN; GAP_DAYS=DATEDIFF(day,COALESCE(PREV_LOGIN,CREATED_ON),wake); keep max-gap row/user; WHERE GAP_DAYS>=45(default gap). Severity reawakening_severity (GAP_DAYS>=180 or ROLE_COUNT>=5 High, >=90 Medium). | count of accounts | security_sql.dormant_reawakening -> LOGIN_HISTORY + USERS + GRANTS_TO_USERS. Toggle-gated. |
| **Unused roles table (no KPI)** | Active roles never assumed by any query in 90d but still granted — revoke candidates (account-wide). | unused_roles(90): ROLES LEFT JOIN DISTINCT ROLE_NAME from 90d QUERY_HISTORY, keep where no query match, exclude PUBLIC; GRANTED_TO_USERS=count of live GRANTS_TO_USERS. Mart-first via mart27_sql.unused_roles_via_fact (FACT_QUERY_ROLE_HOURLY, coverage-guarded). | rows (roles) | run_mart_first: mart27_sql.unused_roles_via_fact -> FACT_QUERY_ROLE_HOURLY (mart) / security_sql.unused_roles -> ROLES x QUERY_HISTORY (live). |
| **Role grants in the window table (no KPI)** | Roles newly granted to users within the page window (account-wide). | recent_role_grants(days): GRANTS_TO_USERS where CREATED_ON>=now-days AND DELETED_ON NULL; capped at 90 days, calendar presets included (v4.606: 'Current year' read its whole range). | rows (grants) | security_sql.recent_role_grants -> ACCOUNT_USAGE.GRANTS_TO_USERS. |
| **Users / Effective paths / High-risk users / Can self-escalate to admin (Effective access paths)** | Recursive expansion of inherited roles: distinct users, total effective role-paths, users whose max privilege RISK_SCORE>=70, and users holding MANAGE GRANTS on some effective role (can grant any role incl. admin to themselves). | security_sql.effective_access recursive role_tree (depth<10, cycle-guarded) joined to privilege_rollup; RISK_SCORE=LEAST(100, OWNERSHIP*10+MANAGE_GRANTS*25+SENSITIVE_PRIVILEGES*20); REACHES_ADMIN if effective role in admin set. logic/security.escalation_flags adds SELF_ESCALATION=MANAGE_GRANTS>0 and ESCALATION_SCORE=clip(risk+depth*4+reaches*25+self*40). v4.606: the KPIs read pre-LIMIT totals over EVERY path (TOTAL_PATH_USERS_WIN, TOTAL_PATHS_WIN, TOTAL_HIGH_RISK_USERS_WIN = users with RISK_SCORE >= 70 on some path, TOTAL_SELF_ESCALATORS_WIN = users with MANAGE GRANTS on some path), not the 3,000 rows kept; the cap keeps each user's own best path first (an escalation path — MANAGE GRANTS or admin reach — then risk), then every escalation path, then risk, so no user or late-alphabet MANAGE GRANTS holder is cut on a tie, and a caption says 'Showing N of M effective paths'. A failed read renders needs-setup (grants not visible) or unavailable with the error, never the old quiet 'did not resolve'. | counts / score | security_sql.effective_access -> ACCOUNT_USAGE GRANTS_TO_USERS + GRANTS_TO_ROLES (recursive). Toggle-gated. security_center.render_effective_access. |
| **Admin grants (90d) / First-ever elevations / Off-hours or weekend (Admin grants timing check)** | Admin-role grants in 90d with timing context: total, count that were first-ever elevations (no prior grant of that role to that user), and count landing off-hours/weekend. | admin_grant_context(90): GRANTS_TO_USERS for admin roles, PRIOR_GRANTS=correlated count of earlier grants of same role+grantee, DOW_ISO, HOUR_OF_DAY. logic/security.grant_anomaly_flags: FIRST_TIME=PRIOR_GRANTS<=0; OFF_HOURS=weekend(DOW in 6,7) OR hour<7 OR hour>=19; SEVERITY HIGH if first-time else MEDIUM if off-hours else INFO. A failed read renders needs-setup (grants not visible) or unavailable with the error (v4.606). | counts | security_sql.admin_grant_context -> ACCOUNT_USAGE.GRANTS_TO_USERS. Toggle-gated. security_center.render_admin_grant_anomalies. |
| **Auditor export pack (rows across N files)** | One-click zip of ten evidence CSVs with a manifest; not a live metric but reports total rows written and failed sheets. | run_batch of ten SQL sheets (dormant_users, users_without_mfa, admin_role_holders, recent_role_grants, failed_logins, expiring_credentials, role_privilege_matrix, unused_roles, direct_role_grants, grant_changes); each actionable sheet gets a leading RECOMMEND column (logic/least_privilege.recommend_for_sheet). v4.606: each sheet is fetched up to its own trailing LIMIT — the MFA, role-grant, failed-login, privilege-matrix, unused-role, direct-grant and grant-change builders read up to 10,000 rows for the pack (other sheets keep their builder LIMIT, e.g. expiring credentials 300) — so run()'s n+1 check flags a sheet that holds more; MANIFEST.txt (and the caption) mark such a sheet TRUNCATED, and the manifest states the span each windowed sheet covers (role_grants_window capped at 90 days, failed_logins_window at 30, calendar presets included). | CSV rows | _export_pack in security.py, multiple security_sql + insights_sql builders -> ACCOUNT_USAGE. |
| **Admins without a user network policy / Admins with one** | Toggle 'Check admin network-policy coverage' (Next-Fifty #9): directly granted elevated-role admins with and without a USER-level network policy. Its caption states whether the policy-reference view lists an account-level network policy (Next-Fifty #43) -- a fact line, not a coverage verdict -- and Snowflake's precedence: a user-level policy overrides the account's, and a security integration's own policy overrides both when an admin signs in through it. | admin_network_policy_coverage: GRANTS_TO_USERS (ELEVATED_ROLES) LEFT JOIN POLICY_REFERENCES NETWORK_POLICY rows on REF_DOMAIN = USER; np_totals COUNT_IF(REF_DOMAIN = 'USER') and COUNT_IF(REF_DOMAIN = 'ACCOUNT') in the same scan. USER_POLICY_REFS = 0 = needs_setup (with the account-policy hint when ACCOUNT_POLICY_REFS > 0), never 'no admin is covered'. A failed read renders by its kind (v4.605): an absent or ungranted POLICY_REFERENCES / GRANTS_TO_USERS view is needs_setup; a missing column (schema drift), a timeout or any other failure renders unavailable with the error, both with the SHOW PARAMETERS LIKE 'NETWORK_POLICY' IN USER <name> check. On this account the view lists one USER-level and one ACCOUNT-level network policy, so the KPIs render. | count | security_sql.admin_network_policy_coverage -> ACCOUNT_USAGE.GRANTS_TO_USERS + POLICY_REFERENCES (probe=True, hourly); policy_coverage.network_policy_caption. |

*Columns:* **USER_NAME / LOGIN_NAME / LAST_SUCCESS_LOGIN / PASSWORD_LOGINS_30D / LAST_PASSWORD_LOGIN (MFA table)** — User identity, last successful login, count of password logins in 30d, and most recent password-login day (proof the MFA gap is real).; **FAILED_ATTEMPTS / DISTINCT_IPS / LAST_ATTEMPT / LAST_ERROR (Failed logins)** — Failed count, distinct source IPs, latest attempt time, and latest error message (live path) or error CATEGORY (fact path — content differs by path).; **ADMIN_ROLE / GRANTED_BY / GRANTED_ON (Privileged holders)** — The admin role held, the role Snowflake attributes as grantor, and the grant timestamp.; **REASON / CATEGORY / ATTEMPTS / USERS / SOURCE_IPS / LAST_SEEN (Failed-login reasons)** — Error text, NETWORK POLICY vs CREDENTIAL/OTHER bucket, attempt count, distinct users and IPs, last occurrence.; **CLIENT_IP / FIRST_SEEN / LOGINS / SUCCESSES / LAST_LOGIN / AUTH_FACTOR (New networks)** — Novel IP for a privileged user, when first observed, total/successful logins from it, last login, and the auth factor used.; **CREDENTIAL_NAME / CREDENTIAL_TYPE / CREATED_ON / EXPIRES_AT / DAYS_TO_EXPIRY / STATUS (Expiring credentials)** — Credential identity/type, creation, expiry (EXPIRATION_DATE aliased EXPIRES_AT), days until expiry, and EXPIRED vs EXPIRING.; **SEVERITY / DAYS_DORMANT / ROLE_COUNT / ROLES / EMAIL (Dormant users)** — Computed severity, days since last login (since account creation for a user who never logged in — v4.606, no longer 9999), number of roles still granted, role list, contact email.; **GAP_DAYS / LAST_ACTIVE_BEFORE / WAKE_LOGIN / AUTH_FACTOR (Reawakening)** — Silence length before the wake login, the prior login timestamp (or account creation if none), the wake login timestamp, and its auth factor.; **ROLE_NAME / CREATED_ON / GRANTED_TO_USERS (Unused roles)** — Unused role, when created, and how many users still hold it.; **GRANTED_ROLE (Role grants window)** — The role newly granted to the user in the window.; **DIRECT_ROLES / EFFECTIVE_ROLES / MAX_RISK / MAX_ESCALATION / SELF_ESCALATE / SENSITIVE_PRIVILEGES (Effective access summary)** — Per-user distinct direct vs inherited roles, max privilege risk score, max escalation score, whether any path can self-escalate, and count of sensitive privileges reached. Detail table adds DIRECT_ROLE, EFFECTIVE_ROLE, DEPTH, ACCESS_PATH, PRIVILEGES, OWNERSHIP_GRANTS, MANAGE_GRANTS, REACHES_ADMIN.; **GRANTED_AT / REASON (Admin grants timing)** — When the admin grant landed and the human-readable anomaly reason (first grant / off-hours / weekend / within normal pattern).

### AI guardrails
AI Trust & Guardrails: per-user Cortex Code behavioral flags (velocity, token outliers, new+heavy) derived from the shared user-day scan, plus optional Cortex Guardrails prompt-flag telemetry. Owner rationale: CoCo spend ~13% of total and growing.

| Metric | Means | Formula | Unit | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **CoCo spend, 7d** | Cortex Code token-credit spend across active users over the trailing 7 complete days, in USD. | credits_to_usd(sum(behavior CREDITS_7D), ai_rate). ai_rate=SETTINGS AI_CREDIT_PRICE_USD (default 2.20). CREDITS_7D = SUM(CREDITS) per user over [today-7, today) from user_behavior. | USD | cortex_sql.cortex_code_user_daily -> CORTEX_CODE_*_USAGE_HISTORY (user-day, shared cache); logic/ai_guardrails.user_behavior + logic/formulas.credits_to_usd/format_usd. NOTE: duplicates Cost > Chargeback & AI (help says so). |
| **Active users, 7d** | Users with any Cortex Code activity in the trailing 7 complete days. | behavior_kpis active_users = len(behavior) (one row per user active in [today-7,today)). | count of users | logic/ai_guardrails.user_behavior/behavior_kpis over cortex_code_user_daily. |
| **Flagged users (+ velocity/outlier/new+heavy breakdown)** | Users hitting at least one behavioral flag; delta splits by flag type. | flagged_users=count(FLAGS!=''). VELOCITY flag: recent daily rate (REQUESTS_7D/7) >= 3x own prior-28d baseline daily AND REQUESTS_7D>=20. TOKEN outlier: robust-z of TOKENS_7D (median/MAD across active users) >= 3.0. new+heavy: first-ever activity within last 7d AND CREDITS_7D>=1.0. Baseline daily = prior-28d requests / days user actually existed (clip 1..28). | count of users | logic/ai_guardrails.py (VELOCITY_RATIO=3, VELOCITY_MIN_REQUESTS=20, TOKEN_Z_FLAG=3, NEW_USER_MIN_CREDITS=1); robust_zscores from logic/anomaly. |
| **Guarded requests, 30d / With guardrail response (%)** | Total Cortex Guardrails-guarded requests in 30d and how many recorded a guardrails response (a heuristic proxy for an intervention), with the % share. | guardrails_daily(30): REQUESTS=COUNT(*), FLAGGED=COUNT_IF(COALESCE(GUARDRAILS_RESPONSE,'')<>''); UI sums each and computes FLAGGED/REQUESTS*100. Probe-gated (v4.603): an absent view / unknown function renders needs_setup 'not readable by this app' (the view exists on this account, so never 'Guardrails is not enabled'); any other failure (a missing column, which a probe read does not log, or a timeout) renders unavailable with the error; zero rows = 'no guardrails-checked requests recorded' (no_data_yet, not clean). START_TIME / GUARDRAILS_RESPONSE are unverified against the view: canary cortex.guardrails_daily FAILs if either is missing. | count and % | cortex_sql.guardrails_daily -> ACCOUNT_USAGE.CORTEX_AI_GUARDRAILS_USAGE_HISTORY (probe=True). Account-wide (no company grain). |

*Columns:* **USER_NAME / REQUESTS_7D / CREDITS_7D / TOKENS_7D (behavior table)** — User and their 7-complete-day request count, credit spend, and token total.; **BASELINE_DAILY** — User's own prior-28d average daily requests, divided by days actually present in the baseline window (avoids false velocity for newly-onboarded users).; **VELOCITY (x baseline)** — recent daily rate / baseline daily rate; NaN for brand-new users (routed to NEW_USER path).; **TOKEN_Z** — Robust z-score (median/MAD) of TOKENS_7D vs the active-user population this week.; **WEEKEND_PCT** — Share of the user's 7d requests that fell on Sat/Sun (coarse off-pattern proxy at day grain).; **NEW_USER / FLAGS** — Whether first activity was within the last 7d; FLAGS = comma-joined fired flags ('' = clean). Sorted flagged-first then by tokens.; **DAY / REQUESTS / FLAGGED (guardrails table)** — Per-day guarded request count and count with a recorded guardrails response.

### Changes
Change evidence: a granular 'who granted/revoked what to whom, when' feed, the DDL/DCL who-changed-what panel with risk scoring and change-registration status, and break-glass admin-role statement activity.

| Metric | Means | Formula | Unit | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Grant changes, Nd / Granted / Revoked (Recent grant changes)** | Grant+revoke events across role->user and privilege->object in the selected window (7/30/90/180d, newest first), split by GRANTED vs REVOKED. | recent_grant_changes(days,company): UNION of GRANTS_TO_USERS (role->user, GRANTED on CREATED_ON, REVOKED on DELETED_ON) and GRANTS_TO_ROLES (privilege->role), excluding self-OWNERSHIP grants (PRIVILEGE='OWNERSHIP' AND GRANTED_BY=GRANTEE_NAME). UI counts TOTAL_CHANGES_WIN / GRANTED_WIN / REVOKED_WIN, pre-LIMIT window totals (v4.606: len and the split of the newest 500 rows, which read exactly 500 on a busy 90/180-day window); the caption says 'Showing the newest N of M changes' when the feed is cut. | count of change events | security_sql.recent_grant_changes -> ACCOUNT_USAGE.GRANTS_TO_USERS + GRANTS_TO_ROLES (tier=historical; lags ~2h). |
| **High-risk change groups / Unregistered groups (DDL/DCL)** | DDL/DCL change groups at CRITICAL/HIGH risk, and object-change groups with no registry entry within 24h. | recent_ddl_changes: grouped by day/user/role/query_type/db/schema; RISK_SCORE = base by query family (DROP/TRUNCATE 90, GRANT/REVOKE 80, POLICY/USER 85, ALTER/RENAME 55, else 30) + 10 if role in admin set + 10 if db ILIKE '%PROD%', LEAST 100; RISK_LEVEL banded (>=90 CRITICAL/>=70 HIGH/>=45 MEDIUM). CHANGE_REGISTRATION REGISTERED if OBJECT_CHANGE_REGISTRY row within +-24h else UNREGISTERED (NOT_APPLICABLE when db/schema NULL). UI counts HIGH_RISK_GROUPS_WIN / UNREGISTERED_GROUPS_WIN (and TOTAL_GROUPS_WIN), pre-LIMIT window totals over the registry-deduped groups (v4.606: they counted the newest 300 groups). Both paths serve the same window, capped at the last 90 days, calendar presets included (v4.606: the live fallback capped at 30 under the same label), and the clean state names it. | count of change groups | security_sql.recent_ddl_changes -> ACCOUNT_USAGE.QUERY_HISTORY + core OBJECT_CHANGE_REGISTRY; fact recent_ddl_changes_fact -> FACT_SECURITY_CHANGE (RISK_SCORE/RISK_LEVEL precomputed). |
| **Change statements/day (stacked chart) / Statements by user (bar)** | Daily count of change statements stacked by change family (Create/Alter/Drop-truncate/Grants/Other), and total statements by top-8 users. | ddl_df.groupby(DAY,CHANGE_KIND).SUM(STATEMENTS) where CHANGE_KIND=_change_kind(QUERY_TYPE); and groupby USER_NAME.SUM(STATEMENTS). charts.daily_stacked_count / charts.bar_count. v4.606: when the window holds more than the 300 groups listed, both charts read an uncapped rollup of the same scope and window (GROUPING SETS (DAY, QUERY_TYPE) and (USER_NAME)) instead of the newest 300 groups, which dropped the oldest days; if that rollup fails they chart the shown groups and say so. | statement counts | Derived in security.py _changes_tab from recent_ddl_changes[_fact] STATEMENTS; full-window rollup security_sql.recent_ddl_changes_rollup / recent_ddl_changes_rollup_fact. |
| **Break-glass role activity table (no KPI)** | Daily statement volume run under ACCOUNTADMIN / SNOW_ACCOUNTADMINS (should hug zero). The read is company-scoped by the statement's user (COMPANY_FOR_USER): v4.606 titles it account-wide only under Company = ALL, else '<company>-classified users', and the clean state names whose statements it checked and the served window (capped at 90 days, calendar presets included). | admin_role_activity(days): COUNT(*) STATEMENTS, COUNT(DISTINCT USER_NAME) by day/role where ROLE_NAME in (ACCOUNTADMIN,SNOW_ACCOUNTADMINS). | statement / user counts per day | security_sql.admin_role_activity -> QUERY_HISTORY (live, all statement types; no fact path). |

*Columns:* **CHANGED_AT / CHANGE / GRANT_TYPE / CHANGED_BY / GRANTEE / WHAT (grant-changes feed)** — Event time; GRANTED vs REVOKED; 'Role -> user' vs 'Privilege -> role'; acting grantor role ('(system)' when internal); recipient user/role; and the role name or 'PRIVILEGE ON object'.; **DAY / USER_NAME / ROLE_NAME / QUERY_TYPE / DATABASE_NAME / SCHEMA_NAME / STATEMENTS / LAST_CHANGE (DDL/DCL)** — Change-group grain: day, actor, executing role, statement type, target db/schema, statement count, and latest statement time.; **RISK_SCORE / RISK_LEVEL / CHANGE_REGISTRATION / CHANGE_KIND / QUERY_ID / LAST_STATEMENT_PREVIEW** — Computed risk 0-100, banded level, registry match state, collapsed change family, latest query id, and 160-char preview of the newest statement.; **ROLE_NAME / STATEMENTS / USERS (break-glass activity)** — Admin role, daily statement count, and distinct users who ran under it.

### Clients
Driver/version inventory from SESSIONS (the 'when do we need to upgrade' sheet) joined in app/logic/client_support.py to Snowflake's own support floor per driver from SYSTEM$CLIENT_VERSION_INFO() (Next-Fifty #34, v4.603). The floors are read live, never hard-coded; the function states a version floor only.

| Metric | Means | Formula | Unit | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Driver families** | Distinct driver families observed connecting in the window. Shown as 'N+' (at least) when the inventory hit run()'s row cap. | client_support.driver_family_count: DRIVER.nunique() excluding '(no client id)'. DRIVER parsed from CLIENT_APPLICATION_ID (trailing version stripped). | count | security_sql.client_drivers -> ACCOUNT_USAGE.SESSIONS. |
| **Driver+version combos** | Distinct driver x version seen in the window (one version reported by two programs counts once). 'N+' when the inventory hit the row cap. | client_support.driver_version_count: distinct (DRIVER, VERSION), '(no client id)' excluded. The builder's rows are DRIVER x VERSION x PROGRAM. | count | security_sql.client_drivers -> ACCOUNT_USAGE.SESSIONS. |
| **Unsupported, yours to upgrade** | Driver versions below Snowflake's minimum supported version that your clients run. '—' when the floor could not be read, when no entry lists a usable minimum (key drift: GET_PATH returns NULL for a renamed key, so the read still succeeds), when none of your versions could be checked against a minimum, or when the inventory hit the row cap. With some of yours not checked (NOT LISTED or NO MINIMUM LISTED) it shows 'N not checked' beneath and is never a green 0. One rule for every support KPI: a version counts only when it was compared with a minimum. | client_support.support_counts at DRIVER x VERSION grain: SUPPORT_CODE = UNSUPPORTED (version_key(VERSION) < version_key(minimumSupportedVersion)) and at least one row WHO_UPGRADES = 'Yours'. checked_yours = your DRIVER x VERSION coded UNSUPPORTED / NEARING_EOS / BELOW_RECOMMENDED / OK (CHECKED_CODES); not_checked_yours = your versioned DRIVER x VERSION coded NOT_LISTED or NOT_COMPARED (NO MINIMUM LISTED: the entry lists no usable minimum, so support_status gives no verdict). Two entries for one driver: the one with a minimum wins (floor_index). | count | client_drivers + security_sql.client_version_info -> SYSTEM$CLIENT_VERSION_INFO() (metadata tier, probe). |
| **Unsupported, Snowflake-run** | Unsupported versions run only by Snowflake's own web app / Snowsight backend or SnowServices ingress: no action, Snowflake upgrades them. '—' when the floor could not be read (or lists no minimum), when none of the Snowflake-run versions could be checked against a minimum, or when the inventory hit the row cap; 'N not checked' beneath (never a green 0) when some of them are NOT LISTED or NO MINIMUM LISTED. | Same, where every row of the version is Snowflake-run (PROGRAM starting 'Snowflake Web App', DRIVER 'Snowsight' or 'SnowServices Ingress'). checked_snowflake / not_checked_snowflake split those versions as checked_yours / not_checked_yours do yours. | count | client_drivers + SYSTEM$CLIENT_VERSION_INFO(). |
| **Nearing end of support** | Your driver versions at or above the minimum but below the version Snowflake lists as nearing end of support. Qualified by the same count as the unsupported KPI: 'N not checked' beneath (never a green 0) when some of yours are NOT LISTED or NO MINIMUM LISTED, '—' when none of yours could be checked. Also '—' when no entry lists a nearing-end-of-support version (minimumNearingEndOfSupportVersion: a renamed key reads empty), with a caption clause saying so. | SUPPORT_CODE = NEARING_EOS and yours (only an entry with a minimum gives it). Band read = client_support.floor_bands(floors).nearing: any entry's value parses (a blank value on one entry is legitimate and skipped). | count | client_drivers + SYSTEM$CLIENT_VERSION_INFO(). |
| **Below recommended** | Your supported driver versions older than Snowflake's recommended version. Qualified like Nearing end of support; '—' when no entry lists a recommended version (recommendedVersion), and the caption then says OK does not mean up to date. | SUPPORT_CODE = BELOW_RECOMMENDED and yours. Band read = floor_bands(floors).recommended. | count | client_drivers + SYSTEM$CLIENT_VERSION_INFO(). |
| **Support caption** | Names each yours-to-upgrade unsupported version with its client and its floor, busiest first, then the Snowflake-run count. 'None ... below the minimum' is said only of versions that were checked: it counts them and names the ones that could not be ('N of your driver versions could not be checked against a minimum (...)'), and never reads clean when none could be. When no entry lists a nearing-end-of-support or recommended version, a closing clause says that band could not be read. When the floor read fails or lists no minimum: why, and that STATUS is not a support verdict. When the inventory hit the row cap: the caption says so and no upgrade list is given. | client_support.support_caption(ann, floor_bands(floors)) / unavailable_caption / capped_caption, built from the MIN_SUPPORTED / RECOMMENDED columns. | text | as above. |
| **Behind-version caption** | How many of your driver versions trail the newest version of the same driver seen in this account. Not shown when the inventory hit the row cap. | client_support.behind_count: distinct (DRIVER, VERSION) with STATUS = 'BEHIND'. STATUS = CASE UPGRADED_BY='SNOWFLAKE' -> 'SNOWFLAKE-RUN'; VKEY NULL (no version) -> 'NO VERSION'; VKEY < MAX(VKEY) OVER (PARTITION BY DRIVER, UPGRADED_BY) -> 'BEHIND'; else 'CURRENT'. VKEY = zero-padded 4-segment key, NULL for '?'. client_drivers has no LIMIT of its own (it had LIMIT 500, which masked truncation), so run()'s row cap is the only cut and sets res.truncated. | count | security_sql.client_drivers -> SESSIONS (the in-account newest; the fallback when the floor cannot be read). |

*Columns:* **DRIVER / VERSION / CLIENT / PROGRAM** — Parsed driver family ('(no client id)' for a NULL or blank CLIENT_APPLICATION_ID, disclosed in a caption and counted in no KPI), version string ('?' when none), friendly client name by PROGRAM prefix (Informatica, Power BI gateway, Power BI (ADBC), AWS Glue, Terraform, Posit, Cortex Code CLI), and the self-reported program ('(not reported)' when the client doesn't set it).; **SUPPORT_STATUS / WHO_UPGRADES / MIN_SUPPORTED / RECOMMENDED** — UNSUPPORTED / NEARING END OF SUPPORT / BELOW RECOMMENDED / OK / NO VERSION / NO MINIMUM LISTED (the entry lists no minimum supported version, so no verdict is given) / NOT LISTED (the function has no entry for this client, or its entry lists no version), 'unavailable' when SYSTEM$CLIENT_VERSION_INFO() could not be read; a Snowflake-run row reads '<status> (Snowflake-run)' in a neutral colour. WHO_UPGRADES = Yours or Snowflake-run. MIN_SUPPORTED / RECOMMENDED are the function's values.; **STATUS / NEWEST_IN_ACCOUNT** — In-account comparison: CURRENT, BEHIND, NO VERSION or SNOWFLAKE-RUN, and the newest version of the same driver among your rows (blank for Snowflake-run and version-less-only drivers).; **USERS / SESSIONS / FIRST_SEEN / LAST_SEEN / SAMPLE_USERS** — Distinct users, session count, first/last session date, and a 160-char sample of user names for that combo. Sort: support status (worst first), yours before Snowflake-run, then sessions; the builder's order (driver, newest version first) when the floor could not be read.

### Egress
Security lenses on data leaving the account: an optional prior-period comparison of outbound destinations (new or spiking), who unloads to stages via COPY INTO, and an opt-in per-event exfiltration score. The outbound-transfer $ story (transfer by destination region) lives on Cost ▸ Spend & Attribution, which owns it; this section links there.

| Metric | Means | Formula | Unit | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **New or spiking destinations (baseline compare)** | Count of destination regions classed NEW or SPIKE vs the prior equal-length period. | egress_baseline(span): CURRENT_GB vs PRIOR_GB over two halves of 2*span days; BEHAVIOR NEW if PRIOR=0&CURRENT>0, SPIKE if CURRENT>PRIOR*2 & CURRENT>=1, else NORMAL; UI counts BEHAVIOR in (NEW,SPIKE). | count of regions | security_sql.egress_baseline -> DATA_TRANSFER_HISTORY. Toggle-gated. |
