| **Unload runs / GB written out / Users unloading** | Successful COPY INTO <location> unload activity in the window: total unload runs, GB written out, and distinct users unloading. | unload_activity: COUNT(*) UNLOADS, GB_OUT=ROUND((SUM(BYTES_WRITTEN)+SUM(BYTES_WRITTEN_TO_RESULT))/1024^3,3), by user/day; QUERY_TYPE='UNLOAD' & SUCCESS. KPIs come from the uncapped security_sql.unload_activity_totals (UNLOADS, GB_OUT, USERS = COUNT(DISTINCT USER_NAME), same scope), falling back to sums over the LIMIT-300 feed only when that read is unavailable. Honors company + database/schema filters. | count / GB / count | security_sql.unload_activity -> ACCOUNT_USAGE.QUERY_HISTORY (UNLOAD only). |

*Columns:* **TARGET_REGION / CURRENT_GB / PRIOR_GB / BASELINE_DAYS / BEHAVIOR (baseline)** — Destination region, GB in the current vs prior period, the span length, and NEW/SPIKE/NORMAL classification.; **UNLOADS / GB_OUT / LAST_UNLOAD / TARGET_LOCATION / SAMPLE_TARGET / QUERY_ID (unload table)** — Unload count, GB written, latest unload time, parsed COPY INTO target, 120-char newest-statement preview, and newest query id.

### Exposure
Outbound data-exposure inventory from SHOW SHARES — which of our objects are exposed to whom — plus masking and row-access policy coverage from Snowflake's policy-reference view (toggle). Account-wide (shares and policy references have no company grain).

| Metric | Means | Formula | Unit | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Outbound shares** | Shares that expose this account's data to other accounts (INBOUND excluded). | summarize_exposure['outbound'] = count(KIND!='INBOUND') over classify_share_exposure(SHOW SHARES). | count of shares | security_sql.show_shares_sql -> SHOW SHARES; logic/exposure.classify_share_exposure/summarize_exposure. |
| **Consumer accounts** | Distinct consumer accounts reached across all outbound shares (same partner counted once). | summarize_exposure['consumer_accounts'] = size of set of uppercased tokens parsed from CONSUMERS across outbound rows (parse_consumers). | count of accounts | logic/exposure over SHOW SHARES 'to' column. |
| **Marketplace listings** | Outbound shares published as a listing — broad by construction, reachable beyond a named consumer list. | count(EXPOSURE=='LISTING'); EXPOSURE=LISTING when listing_global_name is set. | count | logic/exposure.classify_share_exposure. |
| **Broad shares** | Outbound shares reaching 3+ consumer accounts directly. | count(EXPOSURE=='BROAD'); BROAD when CONSUMER_COUNT>=broad_threshold(3). | count | logic/exposure.classify_share_exposure (broad_threshold=3). |
| **Masked columns / Tables and views / Databases / Masking policies** | Next-Fifty #43, toggle 'Check masking and row-access policy coverage'. Distinct masked columns, tables/views with a masked column, databases holding one, and distinct masking policies (column or tag). Information only: not counted in the Decision-queue domain scores. | data_policy_coverage `tot`, every distinct count on a fully qualified name: COUNT(DISTINCT db.schema.object.column), COUNT(DISTINCT db.schema.object) and COUNT(DISTINCT REF_DATABASE_NAME) where POLICY_KIND (UPPER/TRIM, spaces to _) = MASKING_POLICY and REF_ENTITY_DOMAIN <> TAG; COUNT(DISTINCT POLICY_DB.POLICY_SCHEMA.POLICY_NAME) over all masking rows. Read from row 0 (the totals repeat on every row), never summed from the per-database rows. KPIs only when masked columns > 0; tag-only masking = no_data_yet, no masking at all = needs_setup (never clean). Absent/unauthorised view = needs_setup; any other failure = unavailable with the error. | count | security_sql.data_policy_coverage -> ACCOUNT_USAGE.POLICY_REFERENCES (probe=True, hourly, up to 2h behind); logic/policy_coverage.summarize_policy_coverage. |
| **Tag-based masking line** | Masking attached through tags, as its own line. | COUNT(DISTINCT tag db.schema.name), COUNT(DISTINCT REF_DATABASE_NAME), COUNT(DISTINCT policy FQN) where MASKING_POLICY and REF_ENTITY_DOMAIN = TAG. Counts the tags that carry a masking policy, once each even when a tag carries a policy for more than one data type; the columns each tag is set on are not listed. | count | data_policy_coverage; policy_coverage.tag_masking_sentence. |
| **Row-level policies line** | Whether any table or view carries a row-access, projection or aggregation policy; when none does it says so explicitly. | per kind: COUNT(DISTINCT object FQN) and COUNT(DISTINCT policy FQN); kinds with 0 objects are named in one 'No ... policy is in use' sentence. A kind outside the four data-policy kinds and NETWORK_POLICY is listed in OTHER_POLICY_KINDS (an 'Other policy kinds' caption), so a spelling change never reads as 'none in use'. Masking references with a status other than ACTIVE get their own caption. | sentence | data_policy_coverage; policy_coverage.row_policy_sentences / not_active_sentence / other_kinds_sentence. |
| **Environment grouping (SAME / DIFFERS)** | Nested toggle 'Group same-named tables across environments' (shown only when column masking exists): each masked schema.table name in a database family with two or more masked databases, then one line per family naming its databases with no column-level masking reference (a database masked only through a tag is listed too: tags are not traced to columns). Information only, not a gap list. | family = database name up to its last underscore (REGEXP, name-derived; nothing hard-coded); the grouping's family databases = only those with a column-level masking reference (MASKED_FAMILY_DATABASES), so a database with no column-level masking reference appears in neither table; COLUMN_SET = sorted distinct masked-column list per database; SAME if masked in every masked family database on one column set, else DIFFERS. TOTAL_NAMES / DIFFERING_NAMES are window totals taken before the LIMIT 1000. Unmasked databases: the SHOW DATABASES names (the sidebar's cached read: same SQL, metadata tier, no extra scan; the databases the app's role can see) grouped by policy_coverage.db_family (the Python mirror of the SQL rule, one pattern source), listed per family of a masked database, even a family with only one masked database ('ALFA_EDW: no column-level masking reference in ...'; review R2-1: never 'no masked column', since that absence is all the panel knows). When the account has masking tags (PolicyCoverage.masking_tags > 0) a qualifier follows a non-empty list: tag-based masking is not traced to columns, so a database masked only through a tag (or a tag set on the database or schema) can be listed. Every list carries the lag line (followed by the row-limit line when SHOW DATABASES hit its 500-row limit): the policy-reference view can lag up to 2h and the panel's reads of it are cached for up to 1h (the 'hourly' tier; Refresh data clears the cache), so a database masked or cloned in the last 3h can be listed, and the database list is a cached SHOW DATABASES read, so a database created since that read is not listed (policy_coverage.POLICY_VIEW_LAG_SEC + POLICY_READ_CACHE_SEC, review r3; the no-masking needs_setup line gives the same 3h bound). A failed SHOW read = unavailable with the error, no names = no_data_yet, both saying the unmasked databases were not checked; a read at its 500-row limit says databases past it were not checked. | names | security_sql.masking_environment_parity -> POLICY_REFERENCES (probe=True, hourly); policy_coverage.parity_view / parity_counts; security_sql.show_databases_sql -> SHOW DATABASES; policy_coverage.unmasked_family_databases / sibling_lines. |

*Columns:* **SHARE_NAME / KIND / DATABASE / OWNER** — Share name, OUTBOUND vs INBOUND, the database it exposes, and owning role.; **CONSUMER_COUNT / CONSUMERS** — Distinct consumer accounts (Int64, NA when 'to' column unavailable) and their comma-joined names.; **LISTING / EXPOSURE / CREATED** — Marketplace listing global name if any; exposure class (LISTING/BROAD/DIRECT/NO CONSUMERS/INBOUND); and created timestamp.; **DATABASE_NAME / ENVIRONMENT / MASKED_OBJECTS / MASKED_COLUMNS / MASKING_POLICIES / REFS_NOT_ACTIVE (masking by database)** — One row per database with a masked column: distinct fully qualified tables/views and columns, distinct policies used there (a policy used in several databases counts once in each row, so the column does not add up to the account total), references whose status is not ACTIVE; ENVIRONMENT = the name after the last underscore, shown when 2+ masked databases share the rest of the name (a name suffix, not the app's PROD/NONPROD rule).; **DATABASE_FAMILY / SCHEMA_NAME / OBJECT_NAME / PARITY / DATABASES_MASKED / MASKED_FAMILY_DATABASES / COLUMN_SETS / MASKED_IN / NO_MASKING_REF_IN (environment grouping)** — Family stem, schema and object name, SAME/DIFFERS, family databases masking it vs the family's databases with any column masking (a database with no column-level masking reference is not counted; the lines under the table list those), distinct column sets, each masking database with its masked-column count, and family databases with masked columns but none on this name (the table may not exist there).

### Least privilege
Held table data-privileges vs what queries actually exercised over 90 days — revoke/narrow candidates. Coverage-gated on ACCESS_HISTORY density and recency; reports only, revokes nothing. Account-wide, fixed 90d window.

| Metric | Means | Formula | Unit | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **ACCESS_HISTORY coverage caption** | How many of the last 90 days carry access rows and how stale the newest is; verdicts suppressed if coverage_days<28 or newest older than 7 days. | access_evidence_days: COVERAGE_DAYS=COUNT(DISTINCT DATE(QUERY_START_TIME)) over 90d; LATEST_DAYS_AGO=DATEDIFF(day,MAX(ts),now). Thresholds _MIN_COVERAGE_DAYS=28, _MAX_STALENESS_DAYS=7. | days | security_sql.access_evidence_days -> ACCOUNT_USAGE.ACCESS_HISTORY. |
| **Roles reviewed** | Distinct roles with at least one direct table data-privilege resolving to a live table. v4.606 (all four scope counts): the scope read has no LIMIT of its own (it was LIMIT 500, below run()'s row cap, so a cut was never flagged and the least-unused scopes silently fell off); run()'s 5,000-row cap is the only cut, and when it fires each count shows '+' (at least this many) and a caption says 'Showing the N most-unused of M role × schema scopes' (TOTAL_SCOPES_WIN). | summarize_scopes['roles'] = classified['ROLE_NAME'].nunique(). | count | security_sql.grant_scope_usage -> ACCESS_HISTORY x GRANTS_TO_ROLES x TABLE_STORAGE_METRICS; logic/least_privilege. |
| **Unused scopes** | Role×schema footprints where the role touched none of its granted tables in the covered window. | count(VERDICT=='UNUSED'); UNUSED when TOUCHED_TABLES==0. | count of scopes | logic/least_privilege.classify_grant_scopes/summarize_scopes. |
| **Over-broad scopes** | Role×schema footprints granted many tables but exercising a third or fewer. | count(VERDICT=='OVER-BROAD'); OVER-BROAD when GRANTED_TABLES>=4 and TOUCHED_TABLES<=floor(granted/3). | count of scopes | logic/least_privilege.classify_grant_scopes. |
| **Unused table grants** | Total granted tables (by name) that saw no read or write in the covered window. | summarize_scopes['unused_tables'] = sum(UNUSED_TABLES); UNUSED_TABLES=GRANTED_TABLES-TOUCHED_TABLES (touched clamped <= granted). | count of table grants | logic/least_privilege over grant_scope_usage. |
| **Per-table revoke shortlist + REVOKE script** | Table grants on tables no query touched in the window, plus generated copy-paste REVOKE statements (app never runs them). | unused_table_grants(90): grants JOIN tbl LEFT JOIN touched, HAVING MAX(touched)=0; v4.606: worst scope first (most untouched grants per role × database × schema, SCOPE_UNUSED_GRANTS), then role and object, with no LIMIT of its own (it was role-alphabetical LIMIT 500, so a late-alphabet role vanished from the shortlist and its REVOKE script); run()'s row cap is the only cut, disclosed with TOTAL_GRANTS_WIN ('the REVOKE script covers the rows shown'), and a selected scope that filters to nothing says so; logic/least_privilege.revoke_statements formats 'REVOKE <priv> ON TABLE <fqn> FROM ROLE <role>;' for recognized privileges. | rows / SQL statements | security_sql.unused_table_grants -> ACCESS_HISTORY x GRANTS_TO_ROLES x TABLE_STORAGE_METRICS. Toggle-gated. |

*Columns:* **ROLE_NAME / DATABASE_NAME / SCHEMA_NAME (scopes)** — The role and the schema its grant footprint is measured in.; **GRANTED_TABLES / TOUCHED_TABLES / UNUSED_TABLES / USED_PCT / VERDICT** — Distinct granted tables, how many were read/modified, the difference, used percent, and UNUSED/OVER-BROAD/FOCUSED label.; **ROLE_NAME / PRIVILEGE / OBJECT_NAME (unused-grants shortlist)** — Role, the specific data privilege, and the fully-qualified table the privilege is on but unused.

### Trust Center
Snowflake Trust Center scanner posture and movement since the prior snapshot; needs TRUST_CENTER_VIEWER. Account-wide.

| Metric | Means | Formula | Unit | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Scanners tracked / Critical / High / Worsening scanners (delta view)** | Number of scanners tracked, active CRITICAL and HIGH findings, and count of scanners whose finding count increased vs the prior snapshot. | trust_center_delta -> V_SECURITY_TRUST_DELTA. active=CURRENT_COUNT>0; Critical=count(SEVERITY=='CRITICAL' & active); High=count(HIGH & active); worsening=count(COUNT_DELTA>0). Shown only when domain coverage TRUST CENTER == COMPLETE. | counts | security_sql.trust_center_delta -> core V_SECURITY_TRUST_DELTA; gated by security_domain_coverage. |
| **Scanners reporting / Critical / High (fallback findings view)** | Pre-V075 fallback: latest run per scanner with critical/high counts. | trust_center_findings: latest CREATED_ON per SCANNER_ID; UI counts SEVERITY CRITICAL/HIGH over the frame. | counts | security_sql.trust_center_findings -> SNOWFLAKE.TRUST_CENTER.FINDINGS. |

*Columns:* **SCANNER_ID / SCANNER_NAME / SEVERITY** — Scanner identity and its severity.; **CURRENT_COUNT / PRIOR_COUNT / COUNT_DELTA / CHANGE_STATE** — At-risk entity count now vs prior snapshot, the delta, and improving/worsening/steady state.; **SCANNED_AT / SNAPSHOT_DAY / TOTAL_AT_RISK_COUNT** — Scan time, snapshot day; in the fallback view TOTAL_AT_RISK_COUNT is the at-risk entity count for that scanner run.

## Admin
*9 sections · 34 metrics.*

### Context strip (always-on header, above the section picker)
A four-tile session banner so an operator knows which account/role/warehouse they are administering before touching anything.

| Metric | Means | Formula | Unit | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Role** | The Snowflake role of the current session. | CURRENT_ROLE() -> str(row['ROLE']) | text | run(inline SQL) -> session function CURRENT_ROLE(); tier=metadata; no table |
| **Warehouse** | Warehouse bound to the current session (or 'none'). | CURRENT_WAREHOUSE(); rendered str(...) or 'none' | text | inline session SQL CURRENT_WAREHOUSE() |
| **Account** | Current Snowflake account locator. | CURRENT_ACCOUNT() | text | inline session SQL CURRENT_ACCOUNT() |
| **App version** | The OVERWATCH build string this deployment is running. | APP_VERSION constant (not from Snowflake) | text | app.config.APP_VERSION |

### Settings
Every tunable the app reads (rates, budgets, platform-score/governance weights, retention days, alert thresholds) with last-editor provenance, plus a single-key editor that emits and (for operators) runs a MERGE upsert into SETTINGS.

| Metric | Means | Formula | Unit | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Values from: <source>. Rates confirmed 2026-07: $3.68 compute / $2.20 Cortex.** | Caption naming where the effective settings came from (live table vs defaults) and stating the compute/Cortex credit rates. | settings.get('_source'); the two rates are a HARDCODED string literal, NOT read from SETTINGS | text | load_settings(_PAGE) for _source; rate literals hardcoded in admin._settings_tab |
| **Settings rows the app no longer reads (safe to delete)** | Warning listing SETTINGS keys present in the table but absent from DEFAULT_SETTINGS (retired features leave orphan rows). | sorted(set(df['KEY']) - {k in DEFAULT_SETTINGS not starting '_'}) (v4.606: DEPLOY_ACTORS is in DEFAULT_SETTINGS, so it is an editable setting and never flagged here — deleting a populated row would flip every MANAGED warehouse change to MANUAL) | list of keys | app-side set difference of SETTINGS.KEY vs app.config.DEFAULT_SETTINGS |
| **Change a setting -> MERGE (upsert) SQL** | Generated MERGE statement previewed and, behind a type-to-confirm gate (the setting key), executed for operators. An upsert, so a DEFAULT_SETTINGS key no migration seeds (e.g. SCORE_PTS_* / GOV_PTS_* / FORECAST_ENGINE) is inserted instead of a 0-row UPDATE silently losing the edit. | MERGE INTO SETTINGS t USING (SELECT <key> AS KEY, <value> AS VALUE) s ON t.KEY = s.KEY WHEN MATCHED THEN UPDATE SET VALUE = s.VALUE, UPDATED_AT = CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(), UPDATED_BY = identity_sql() WHEN NOT MATCHED THEN INSERT (KEY, VALUE, UPDATED_AT, UPDATED_BY) | SQL / mutation | execute_statement -> core SETTINGS; new value typed via _setting_value_input |

*Columns:* **KEY** — Setting name (mart_sql.settings -> SETTINGS.KEY).; **VALUE** — Stored value as a STRING (numbers/dates are string-typed in SETTINGS).; **UPDATED_AT** — Timestamp of the last edit (SETTINGS.UPDATED_AT), account-time.; **Updated by** — SETTINGS.UPDATED_BY resolved to a display name by with_user_names().

### Migrations & freshness
Contract check of applied schema migrations against what this build expects, the optional Flyway ledger, and per-source loader freshness with a stale-source diagnoser.

| Metric | Means | Formula | Unit | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **N migration(s) not yet applied / All N migrations this build expects are applied** | Which expected migrations are not yet in SCHEMA_VERSION (one 'VNNN — description' row each, to apply in order); the clean state 'All N migrations this build expects are applied (V001–Vtip), and none newer.' shows only when nothing is missing and nothing is newer. When the database is ahead of the build, a caption reads 'Applied but newer than this build: <span> — see the redeploy warning above' (#26, v4.599). | [V{n} for n,name in _EXPECTED_MIGRATIONS if n not in applied]; applied = {int(VERSION)} from SCHEMA_VERSION, unioned with the header banner's fresh read; N = len(_EXPECTED_MIGRATIONS) (the migrations this build knows; not pinned here); ahead = applied versions this build does not know (deploy_health.schema_drift: applied - expected) | list / count | mart_sql.schema_version -> SCHEMA_VERSION.VERSION vs admin.py _EXPECTED_MIGRATIONS dict |

*Columns:* **VERSION** — Applied migration number (SCHEMA_VERSION.VERSION).; **DESCRIPTION** — Migration description (SCHEMA_VERSION.DESCRIPTION; widened to VARCHAR(4000) in V071).; **APPLIED_AT** — When the migration was applied (SCHEMA_VERSION.APPLIED_AT).; **INSTALLED_RANK** — Flyway ledger apply order (flyway_schema_history.installed_rank), newest first.; **VERSION (Flyway)** — Flyway version string (flyway_schema_history.version).; **DESCRIPTION (Flyway)** — Flyway migration description.; **INSTALLED_BY** — Role/user that applied it (flyway_schema_history.installed_by).; **INSTALLED_ON** — Flyway apply timestamp.; **EXECUTION_MS** — Flyway execution_time; column named _MS so styled_table humanizes it as a duration.; **SUCCESS** — Flyway success flag.; **SOURCE_NAME** — Loader source / mart name (SOURCE_FRESHNESS_STATE or MART_SOURCE_FRESHNESS).; **LAST_LOAD_TS** — Last successful loader write time for the source.; **ROW_COUNT** — Row count in the source at last load (0 is legitimate for a source with no events).; **HOURS_SINCE_LOAD** — Hours since LAST_LOAD_TS = DATEDIFF('minute',LAST_LOAD_TS,CURRENT_TIMESTAMP())/60.0; NULL when never loaded (state path); humanized to h. Diagnose flags >26h.

### Setup progress
One onboarding checklist consolidating the scattered 'Apply VNNN' walls: migrations applied (one 'N of M applied' rollup), marts loading, and the budget/contract/route settings that light up alerting and forecasting.

| Metric | Means | Formula | Unit | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Database migrations** | Done when SCHEMA_VERSION is readable, nothing in _EXPECTED_MIGRATIONS is missing and nothing applied is newer than this build; a database ahead of the build with nothing missing is Partial, never Done (#26), and its FIX adds 'redeploy the app from the revision the newer migrations came from (snow streamlit deploy --replace)'. Unknown (v4.605) when the read fails other than by absence (a timeout, drift): 'SCHEMA_VERSION could not be read, so which migrations are applied is unknown', FIX 'Retry. The read failed; this is not a setup gap.' -- or, for a missing column (schema drift, which a retry never clears), 'Schema drift: a column this build reads is missing. Apply the missing migrations, or redeploy the app if the database is ahead of it ...'. An absent table stays Pending ('nothing applied yet'). | done = bool(applied) and not drift.missing and not drift.ahead; partial = bool(drift.ahead) and not drift.missing; detail = '{known - missing} of {known} applied[; N missing][; N newer than this build — redeploy the app]', known = len(_EXPECTED_MIGRATIONS) | status | mart_sql.schema_version -> SCHEMA_VERSION (second read, key setup_schema_version) |
| **Marts loading** | Done when no tracked source has a NULL LAST_LOAD_TS (never-loaded); Partial when some do. Pending with 'SOURCE_FRESHNESS_STATE is empty' when the read returns no rows; Unknown (v4.605) when it fails other than by absence. | _never = count(LAST_LOAD_TS IS NULL); done=_never==0 | status/count | mart_sql.source_freshness_state -> SOURCE_FRESHNESS_STATE |
| **Monthly budget set** | Done when MONTHLY_BUDGET_USD is set and not 0. | done = bool(_budget) and _budget not in ('0','0.0') | status (USD setting) | load_settings -> SETTINGS.MONTHLY_BUDGET_USD |
| **Contract configured** | Done when both CONTRACT_START_DATE and non-zero CONTRACT_CREDITS are set. | done = bool(start) and bool(credits) and credits not in ('0','0.0') | status | load_settings -> SETTINGS.CONTRACT_START_DATE, CONTRACT_CREDITS |
| **Alert routes configured** | Done when >=1 enabled ALERT_ROUTES row exists. A readable table reads 'N enabled route(s)' (0 included; this account has 1); an absent one 'routes table not readable'; any other failed read is Unknown (v4.605). | _n_routes = count(ENABLED in TRUE/1); done=_n_routes>0 | count | mart_sql.alert_routes -> ALERT_ROUTES.ENABLED |
| **N setup item(s) still pending** | Rollup banner counting Pending and Partial rows. | pending = count(STATUS in ('Pending','Partial')) | count | app-side over the assembled rows |
| **N setup item(s) could not be checked** | Red unavailable line (v4.605) counting Unknown rows: a read failed, which is not a setup gap. It ends 'Retry.', or, when a failed read is a missing column, says a retry will not clear that schema drift; its Error detail lists each failed read as '<SOURCE>: <error>' (a probe read leaves a missing column unlogged, so this is its only record). 'Setup complete' shows only when no row is Pending, Partial or Unknown. | unknown = count(STATUS = 'Unknown') | count | app-side over the assembled rows |

*Columns:* **STEP** — Checklist item label.; **STATUS** — Done / Partial / Pending / Unknown (a failed read, v4.605).; **DETAIL** — Evidence string (e.g. 'N of M applied', 'MONTHLY_BUDGET_USD = ...').; **FIX** — Remediation text; blank when Done; when Unknown, 'Retry' for a timeout or other failure and the schema-drift fix (apply migrations / redeploy) for a missing column.

### Metrics (Cost metric registry)
The single semantic contract for every cost number in the app — every registered metric, with method/grain/window/source/timezone/latency/formula-version, so a reader knows exactly what a dollar means and how it was derived.

| Metric | Means | Formula | Unit | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **N registered metrics · methods: BILLED, METERED, MEASURED, ALLOCATED, ESTIMATED** | Count and the method vocabulary; the table is documentation-grade (drift test pins entries but cannot auto-discover an unregistered KPI). | len(mr.as_rows()); methods = mr.METHODS | count | app.logic.metric_registry.METRICS (pure Python constant, no Snowflake) |

*Columns:* **Metric** — Human label of the cost number (Metric.label).; **Method** — How derived: BILLED (billed credits), METERED (exact usage, idle in), MEASURED (attributed compute, idle out), ALLOCATED (share-based estimate), ESTIMATED (bytes/credits x rate).; **Grain** — The dimensional grain (e.g. account/day/service).; **Window** — Time basis (trailing-complete-days, vs-prior-half-window, mtd, calendar-month, rolling-daily, point-in-time, projection, n/a).; **Partial day** — Whether today's partial day is excluded/included/n-a.; **Unit** — USD/currency/credits/days/percent/count/ratio/bytes.; **Source** — The table/mart/view + column the metric reads.; **Timezone** — UTC or America/Chicago (account tz).; **Latency** — Data lag descriptor (e.g. 'up to 24h', '~8h').; **Formula ver** — The formula version that produced the definition.; **Owner** — platform/finops/dataeng — who owns the definition.; **Notes** — Coverage caveats and exclusions in prose.

### App self-cost
What OVERWATCH itself spends on its shared warehouse WH_ALFA_ADMIN over 14 days, split by query tag into interactive-app vs loader/task traffic.

| Metric | Means | Formula | Unit | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **App queries (14d)** | Count of the app's own statements on WH_ALFA_ADMIN in 14 days (the INTERACTIVE APP rows: statements Streamlit-in-Snowflake tagged as this app). '—' when none. | total = sum(APP_QUERIES) over WORKLOAD = 'INTERACTIVE APP'; APP_QUERIES = COUNT(*) per DAY,WORKLOAD | count | mart_sql.app_self_cost(14) -> ACCOUNT_USAGE.QUERY_HISTORY WHERE WAREHOUSE_NAME='WH_ALFA_ADMIN', GROUP BY DAY, tag-split |
| **Failed** | Total failed queries on the warehouse in 14d (both workloads). | failed = sum(FAILED); FAILED = SUM(IFF(EXECUTION_STATUS<>'SUCCESS',1,0)) | count | same builder; delta_color inverse when >0 |

*Columns:* **DAY** — DATE(START_TIME) of the queries (last 14 complete-ish days from CURRENT_DATE).; **WORKLOAD** — mart_sql._self_workload_sql: 'APP RUNTIME (SiS)' for the EXECUTE STREAMLIT session statement (tested first), 'INTERACTIVE APP' for statements carrying the app's SiS tag (common.app_self_sql), else 'TASKS / ALERTS / OTHER'.; **APP_QUERIES** — COUNT(*) of queries in that DAY/WORKLOAD bucket.; **ELAPSED_SEC** — SUM(TOTAL_ELAPSED_TIME)/1000 seconds; column named _SEC so styled_table humanizes it (h/m/s).; **FAILED** — Count of non-SUCCESS queries in that bucket.

### Performance
Prove (or disprove) the app is fast from its own persisted evidence: per-page SLO scorecard, this-session statement stats, the app's own slowest builders (telemetry, with an optional QUERY_HISTORY GB-scan), page adoption, and fleet-wide slow/failed fetches with per-page tuning targets.

| Metric | Means | Formula | Unit | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Real statement-timeout ceiling (Production statement-timeout ceiling)** | The statement timeout every app READ on the app warehouse runs against: the lower non-zero of the warehouse's STATEMENT_TIMEOUT_IN_SECONDS and the session's (the account's, unless a user or session sets its own -- the tile reads the warehouse and account values only); the per-tier 30/120/180s values are not applied on owner's-rights SiS. V002 set 300 s (5m) at install; the value in force is READ, never assumed (v4.603, #33 D2: on this account WH_ALFA_ADMIN's cancels fire at 1800 s). | SHOW PARAMETERS LIKE 'STATEMENT_TIMEOUT_IN_SECONDS' IN WAREHOUSE WH_ALFA_ADMIN (VALUE, LEVEL; '' = Snowflake default, 172800 s) + ops_sql.account_stmt_timeout_sql (IN ACCOUNT, the posture panel's read; derived from the warehouse row by stmt_timeout.derive_account_timeout when unreadable) -> stmt_timeout.effective_timeout_s, humanized. 0 at one level = no limit there; '0 = 7-day max' (604800 s, 168h) only when both are 0. The delta names the cap source ('capped by the account value' when the account's is lower). Account value unknown (its read failed and the warehouse sets its own value): the warehouse's own value, qualified 'the session/account value, if lower, caps reads'. A failed warehouse read renders 'unavailable' with its error (the ceiling in force is unknown), never a guessed value. | seconds | admin._stmt_timeout_ceiling / _ceiling_tile (metadata tier, probe) |
| **Passing pages / Failing pages / Watch / insufficient** | Counts of pages by SLO verdict. | count(SLO_STATE=='PASS'\|'FAIL'\|isin('WATCH','INSUFFICIENT')); SLO_STATE = INSUFFICIENT if render n<20, FAIL if p95_render>target(8000ms) or est_failure_pct>1.0, WATCH if cache_hit<60, else PASS | count | mart_sql.app_performance_slo(7) -> APP_USAGE (RENDER_MS p95) + APP_QUERY_TELEMETRY (failure/cache), target from ALERT_CONFIG OPS_SLOW_RENDER |
| **Statements this session** | Number of query fetches recorded in this Streamlit session. | served = len(query_telemetry()) | count | app.core.query.query_telemetry() in-session buffer |
| **Served in <50ms** | Approximate cache-hit rate — share of session fetches answered from Streamlit cache. | fast/served*100; fast = count(elapsed_ms<50) | percent | query_telemetry() session buffer |
| **Failed (session)** | Count of failed fetches this session. | sum(~telemetry['ok']) | count | query_telemetry() |
| **Next tuning targets -> PAIN** | Top-5 pages ranked by estimated total fleet seconds waited, sample-reweighted; the telemetry's pick of what to optimize. | PAIN = EST_WAIT_S rounded; filter PAIN>0, sort desc, head(5); EST_WAIT_S = SUM(ELAPSED_MS/1000/SAMPLE_PROB) excl batch_wall | seconds | mart_sql.telemetry_by_page(7) -> APP_QUERY_TELEMETRY |
| **Fixes executed / copied / failed** | Remediation acceptance counts over 90d. | COUNT(*) FROM REMEDIATION_LOG WHERE STATUS in EXECUTED/COPIED/FAILED and EXECUTED_AT within 90d | count | mart_sql.acceptance_funnel(90) -> REMEDIATION_LOG |
| **Savings est -> verified / rejected** | Savings-ledger state counts over 90d. | COUNT(*) FROM SAVINGS_LEDGER WHERE STATE in ESTIMATED/VERIFIED/REJECTED and CREATED_AT within 90d | count | mart_sql.acceptance_funnel(90) -> SAVINGS_LEDGER |
| **Verified savings (90d)** | Dollar sum of verified savings in the window. | ROUND(SUM(VERIFIED_USD),2) FROM SAVINGS_LEDGER WHERE STATE='VERIFIED' AND CREATED_AT within 90d — note: windowed on CREATED_AT, not VERIFIED_AT | USD | mart_sql.acceptance_funnel -> SAVINGS_LEDGER.VERIFIED_USD |

*Columns:* **PAGE / RENDER_SAMPLES / P95_RENDER_MS / RENDER_TARGET_MS / EST_FETCHES / EST_FAILURE_PCT / CACHE_HIT_PCT / FAILURE_TARGET_PCT / CACHE_TARGET_PCT / SLO_STATE** — SLO scorecard: page; complete first-paint samples (min 20); APPROX_PERCENTILE(RENDER_MS,0.95) humanized as ms; target (ALERT_CONFIG OPS_SLOW_RENDER x1000, default 8000ms); sample-reweighted fetch count; sample-reweighted failure %; weighted cache-hit %; constants 1.0 and 60.0 targets; PASS/FAIL/WATCH/INSUFFICIENT verdict.; **PAGE / QUERY_KEY / RUNS / EST_RUNS / FAILS / MEDIAN_S / P95_S / EST_WAIT_S / CACHE_HIT_PCT / NEWEST / SLOWEST_QUERY_ID** — App's own slowest builders (app_statement_stats_telemetry): call-site key; persisted run count; EST_RUNS=SUM(1/SAMPLE_PROB); non-OK count; MEDIAN/APPROX_PERCENTILE(ELAPSED_MS,0.95)/1000 humanized; EST_WAIT_S=SUM(ms/1000/prob); weighted cache-hit; newest AT; slowest server QUERY_ID for the Snowsight profile link. Ranked EST_WAIT_S desc, LIMIT 30.; **QUERY_PARAMETERIZED_HASH / SAMPLE_TEXT / RUNS / FAILS / MEDIAN_S / P95_S / AVG_GB_SCANNED / SLOWEST_QUERY_ID** — Optional ACCOUNT_USAGE scan (app_statement_stats): tagged WH_ALFA_ADMIN statements grouped by parameterized hash; MEDIAN/APPROX_PERCENTILE(TOTAL_ELAPSED_TIME)/1000; AVG(BYTES_SCANNED)/1024^3 GB — the one figure telemetry can't record. Ranked P95_S, LIMIT 30.; **PAGE / VISITS / USERS / WAU / LAST_VISIT** — Page adoption (app_usage_summary 30d): visits (EVENT_KIND='page_visit'); distinct users; distinct users in last 7d; MAX(AT).; **PAGE / QUERY_KEY / SLOW_OR_FAILED / FAILURES / P50_MS / P95_MS / ROLES_AFFECTED / NEWEST / SLOWEST_QUERY_ID** — Fleet slow/failed fetches (fleet_query_stats 7d): the table persists every >=2s or failed fetch plus a ~2% healthy sample, and the read keeps only the >=2s-or-failed rows (WHERE NOT OK OR ELAPSED_MS >= 2000; v4.606 — the healthy sample used to count as slow and pull P50/P95 down; the per-page slow-key drills read the same way); a failed read renders unavailable with the error, needs-setup (V021 + roles.sql) only for a true absence; count; failures; APPROX_PERCENTILE 0.5/0.95 ms; distinct roles; newest; slowest server QUERY_ID. Ranked P95_MS, LIMIT 40.; **PAGE / FETCHES / EST_TRUE_FETCHES / EST_WAIT_S / P95_S / CACHE_HIT_PCT / FAILED / SLOW_2S / AVG_BATCH / TRUNCATED_N** — Fleet telemetry by page (telemetry_by_page 7d): persisted count; SUM(1/SAMPLE_PROB) true estimate; ranking EST_WAIT_S; tail-sample p95 (reads high); weighted cache-hit; honest failed/>=2s counts; AVG batch size; truncated count.; **Next tuning targets: PAGE / PAIN / P95_S / SLOW_2S / FAILED** — Top-5 pages by PAIN=EST_WAIT_S with p95 as the severity lens; selectable to drill into that page's slow keys.; **Usage events: EVENT_KIND / EVENTS / USERS / LAST_SEEN** — usage_event_summary(30): COALESCE(EVENT_KIND,'page_visit'); event count; distinct users; MAX(AT). page_visit dominates by design; interaction kinds are the effectiveness signal.

### Canary
Drift and truth checks: run every registered SQL builder against the live account (execute or EXPLAIN), reconcile the object-cost ledger and mart totals vs live ACCOUNT_USAGE, score whether the alert fire-drill reached a human, and flag metering days restated after close.

| Metric | Means | Formula | Unit | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **N registered statements / All N passed / K failed** | Canary outcome banner; a FAIL means an app query no longer compiles (drift), a GAP is a declared account-feature absence. One conditional exception (Admin > Canary names it): cortex.code_token_types FAILs, its ERROR naming TOKENS_GRANULAR as an invalid identifier, on accounts whose Cortex Code views predate that optional column. That FAIL is expected only if the CoCo efficiency review has never shown token types on the account; if it has, the column was renamed or dropped: drift, fix cortex_sql.cortex_code_token_types. | per builder run(EXPLAIN or 1-row); STATUS=PASS if ok, GAP if error_kind in (absent,unknown_function) and name in EXPECTED_GAPS, else FAIL | count | app.data.canary.CANARIES/EXPECTED_GAPS run live, probe=True; failures logged to APP_ERROR_LOG. v4.603 adds 6 entries: the probe=True Cortex reads cortex.guardrails_daily / code_token_types / quota_access_block_history / app_self_cost, declared gaps, so only absence is a GAP and a missing column (an invalid identifier, which the pages' probe reads never log) FAILs; security.client_version_info (SYSTEM$CLIENT_VERSION_INFO(), the Clients support floor; a declared gap, so an unknown function reads GAP); ops.warehouse_timeout_impact (the alert drawer's one-warehouse impact read; not a declared gap, so absence FAILs); v4.604 adds 5 entries, none a declared gap: security.data_policy_coverage, security.masking_environment_parity and security.admin_network_policy_coverage (#43, POLICY_REFERENCES reads, so an absent view or renamed column FAILs); insights.warehouse_cluster_use (#38, the cluster-cap read; CLUSTER_NUMBER is a standard QUERY_HISTORY column, so absence FAILs); chargeback.company_allin_showback (#42, core OVERWATCH marts, so absence FAILs); v4.606 adds 4 entries, none a declared gap: recheck.queued_minutes and recheck.spill_gb (the alert drawer's queued-time / remote-spill re-checks, now on FACT_QUERY_HOURLY's QUEUED_SEC_SUM / SPILL_REMOTE_GB), security.recent_ddl_changes_rollup and security.recent_ddl_changes_rollup_fact (the Changes tab's uncapped DDL chart rollups, live and fact) |
| **Mart drift beyond/within ±5% (worst)** | Largest absolute mart-vs-live drift; >5% means chargeback/exec numbers are off until backfill. | worst = max(abs(DRIFT_PCT)); STATE = OK<=2, WARN<=5, BAD>5 | percent | mart_sql.mart_vs_live_recon -> FACT_METERING_DAILY vs METERING_DAILY_HISTORY (28d), FACT_QUERY_HOURLY vs QUERY_HISTORY (7d) |
| **Drill streak** | Consecutive months where the synthetic CRITICAL fire-drill was both DELIVERED and ACKED. | streak_months from drill_report (v4.606): one outcome per CALENDAR month in account time (passed if any drill that month was both delivered and acked), counted backward from the month whose drill is due now (drill.due_month: this month once its 1st-of-month 09:00 CT run plus a 1h grace has passed, else last month; a newer month that already holds a drill starts the walk instead), so a due month with no drill reads 0; a failed month or a MISSING month (a suspended or torn-down drill task writes no row) ends the streak — it used to count passing rows, so Sep + Jul passes with no August drill read 2 | months | app.logic.drill.drill_report over mart_sql.drill_history(14) -> ALERT_EVENTS WHERE RULE_ID='OPS_ALERT_DRILL' |
| **Last drill delivered** | Whether the most recent drill got a NOTIFIED_AT stamp. | last['delivered'] = notna(NOTIFIED_AT) | yes/NO | drill_report -> ALERT_EVENTS |
| **Time to ack** | Minutes from RAISED_AT to ACK_AT on the last drill (MTTA). | mtta_min = (ACK_AT - RAISED_AT).total_seconds()/60; humanized | minutes | drill_report -> ALERT_EVENTS |

*Columns:* **CHECK_NAME / LEDGER_CREDITS / SOURCE_CREDITS / DELTA_CREDITS / DELTA_PCT** — Object-cost recon (cost_sql.object_cost_recon 7d): query-arms+residual vs QUERY_ATTRIBUTION_HISTORY (COMPUTE+QAS), and each maintenance arm vs its serverless history (AUTO_CLUSTERING/MV_REFRESH/SEARCH_OPT/SERVERLESS_TASK/SNOWPIPE); DELTA=ledger-source; DELTA_PCT formatted %.2f%%; window ends 2 days ago.; **CHECK / EXECUTION_STATUS / ROWS / ERROR** — Canary result rows: builder name; SUCCESS/FAILED/GAP (relabeled from PASS/FAIL/GAP); rows returned by the 1-row probe; truncated error text.; **CHECK_NAME / FACT_VALUE / LIVE_VALUE / DRIFT_PCT / STATE** — Mart reconciliation: 'Billed credits (28d)' and 'Query count (7d)'; mart total vs live ACCOUNT_USAGE; DRIFT_PCT=100*(fact-live)/live formatted %.2f%%; STATE OK/WARN/BAD by ±2/±5.; **RAISED_AT / NOTIFIED_AT / ACK_AT / STATUS / TITLE** — Fire-drill events (drill_history 14mo): raised time; notify-chain stamp; human ack time; event status; title. Resolve drills as EXPECTED so they're excluded from rule precision.; **DAY / CREDITS_BILLED / LAST_LOADED_AT / RESTATED_HOURS_AFTER_CLOSE** — Restated days (metering_restatements 60d): metering day; SUM(CREDITS_BILLED); MAX(LOAD_TS); hours after day-close (DAY+1 midnight) that the row last changed; HAVING >=48h.

### Errors & telemetry
Operability surface: an access self-check probing every privileged source, this-session error buffer, the persisted cross-session error log (grouped into families), and this-session query telemetry with a cache-refresh button.

| Metric | Means | Formula | Unit | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **All N sources reachable / K source(s) blocked** | Access self-check result across 7 privileged probes. | blocked = count(STATUS=='BLOCKED'); each probe runs a 1-row (or SHOW) read and records OK/BLOCKED + fix. v4.606: the probes read on the 30-second live tier (were the 4-hour metadata tier), so a grant revoked after an earlier passing click no longer reads 'All sources reachable' | count | admin.py _access_self_check inline probes: ACCOUNT_USAGE.DATABASES/LOGIN_HISTORY/CREDENTIALS/DATA_TRANSFER_HISTORY, TRUST_CENTER.FINDINGS, DBA_MAINT_DB.OVERWATCH.SETTINGS, SHOW WAREHOUSES |
| **No errors recorded / Error sink empty** | Session vs persisted error presence. | error_buffer() emptiness; sink.empty | boolean | app.core.errors.error_buffer(); mart_sql.app_error_log(100) -> APP_ERROR_LOG |
| **No queries have run yet this session** | Session telemetry emptiness gate. | query_telemetry().empty | boolean | query_telemetry() |

*Columns:* **SOURCE / STATUS / FIX / ERROR** — Access self-check: probe name; OK/BLOCKED; the exact GRANT to run when blocked; truncated error.; **at / ref / page / type / message** — Recent app errors this session (error_buffer): timestamp, the copyable error reference (#46), page, exception type, message.; **PAGE / FAMILY / COUNT / FIRST_SEEN / LAST_SEEN / VIEWERS / LAST_BUILD** — Persisted error log grouped: FAMILY = ERROR_TYPE + first 60 chars of ERROR_MESSAGE; VIEWERS = distinct viewers parsed from CONTEXT (app_telemetry.parse_error_context, #50; '—' when unknown, i.e. rows logged before 4.599); LAST_BUILD = the build of the newest row in the family that carries one (parsed from CONTEXT); families, counts and viewers cover the newest 100 logged errors; COUNT=size; FIRST/LAST_SEEN = min/max LOGGED_AT. Sorted by LAST_SEEN desc.; **LOGGED_AT / PAGE / ERROR_TYPE / ERROR_MESSAGE / CONTEXT / ROLE_NAME** — Raw persisted error rows (app_error_log 100 -> APP_ERROR_LOG), newest first, in the expander.; **query telemetry: at / page / query_key / elapsed_ms / ok / query_id** — This-session fetches (query_telemetry); a Snowsight profile column is added from query_id. Cache hits have no server query/profile.
